which is a function of all the relevant kinematic variables:

\widehat{\mathcal{M}}\big[\hat{h}_{\hat{\sigma}_{1}}\hat{h}_{\hat{\sigma}_{2}}\hat{h}_{\hat{\sigma}_{3}}\hat{h}_{\hat{\sigma}_{4}}\big]=\frac{\,\hat{\kappa}^{2}\,}{\,\hat{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{u}~}\!\!\sum_{\hat{\lambda}_{k},\hat{\lambda}^{\prime}_{k}}\!\!\!\Big(\!\prod_{k=1}^{4}\!\hskip-0.85358ptC^{\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\sigma}_{k}}_{\hat{\lambda}_{k}\hat{\lambda}^{\prime}_{k}}\Big)K_{1234}\!\times\!K_{1^{\prime}2^{\prime}3^{\prime}4^{\prime}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(37)

where the factor
K_{1234}\equiv K_{\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}\lambda_{3}\lambda_{4}}

is given by

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109ptK=

\displaystyle\ \hat{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{t}\hskip 0.85358pt(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})\hskip 0.85358pt(\hat{\zeta}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})+\hat{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{u}\hskip 0.85358pt(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})\hskip 0.85358pt(\hat{\zeta}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})+\hat{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{u}\hskip 0.85358pt(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})\hskip 0.85358pt(\hat{\zeta}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})

\displaystyle\ -2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{s}\,[(\hat{p}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})(\hat{p}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})+(\hat{p}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})(\hat{p}_{4}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})+(\hat{p}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})(\hat{p}_{4}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{1})(\hat{\zeta}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})

\displaystyle\ +(\hat{p}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})(\hat{p}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{1})(\hat{\zeta}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})]-2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{t}\,[(\hat{p}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})(\hat{p}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})+(\hat{p}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})(\hat{p}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})

\displaystyle\ +(\hat{p}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{1})(\hat{p}_{4}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})(\hat{\zeta}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})+(\hat{p}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{1})(\hat{p}_{4}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})(\hat{\zeta}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})]-2\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{u}\big[(\hat{p}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})(\hat{p}_{4}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})

\displaystyle\ +(\hat{p}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})(\hat{p}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})(\hat{\zeta}_{1}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})+(\hat{p}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{2})(\hat{p}_{4}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{1})(\hat{\zeta}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})+(\hat{p}_{3}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{4})(\hat{p}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{1})(\hat{\zeta}_{2}\!\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{3})\big]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(38)

and the factor

K_{1^{\prime}2^{\prime}3^{\prime}4^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358ptK_{\lambda_{1}^{\prime}\lambda_{2}^{\prime}\lambda_{3}^{\prime}\lambda_{4}^{\prime}}
takes the same form as Eq.(
3.1
). As can be checked, the four-point 5d massless graviton
scattering amplitude
given by Eq.(
36
) or Eqs.(
37
)-(
3.1
)
agrees with that of the direct Feynman diagram calculation. It also agrees with the field theory limit of
the four-point genus-zero closed string amplitude
Schwarz:1982
.

Then, under the toroidal compactification of
S^{1}
and
from the
N
-point massive double-copy formula (
24
),
we can deduce the four-point massive KK graviton
scattering amplitude as follows:

\hskip-28.45274pt\mathcal{M}\!\left[h_{\sigma_{1}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}h_{\sigma_{2}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}h_{\sigma_{3}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}h_{\sigma_{4}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\right]=\,-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{16}\sum_{j}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\!\!\Big(\!\prod_{k=1}^{4}\!\hskip-0.85358ptC_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\Big)\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{~\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}(\lambda_{k})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}^{\mathsf{P}}_{j}(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})~}{s_{j}-M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}}\,,

(39)

where
\{C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\}

denote the coefficients of the polarization tensor
of the
k
-th external KK graviton state as
given by Eqs.(
25
)-(
26
).
We can apply the generalized gauge transformation
(
34
) to the double-copied KK graviton scattering amplitude
(
39
) and require it to be gauge-invariant. From this we deduce the following two conditions:

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-0.85358pt=0\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~~\sum_{j}\!\left(s_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}\hskip-0.85358pt\right)\hskip-0.85358pt=0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(40)

where
\hskip 0.85358ptj\hskip-1.42262pt\in\!\{s,t,u\}\hskip 0.85358pt
. The first condition in Eq.(
40
) is the massive
kinematic Jacobi identity for the numerators of the four-point
KK gauge boson amplitude (
32
)
and can be inferred from the massless
kinematic Jacobi identity in the second formula of
Eq.(
30
) by using the shifting method
of Sec.
2.2
. The second condition in Eq.(
40
) is also important
and can be derived from the 5d massless kinematic condition

\hskip 0.85358pt\sum_{j}\hskip-1.42262pt\hat{s}_{j}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt
in
Eq.(
201
) by using the shifting formula
(
12a
). Thus, according to Eq.(
201
),
we have the sum of Mandelstam variables for the
four KK gauge boson scattering,

\hskip 0.85358pt\sum_{j}\hskip-0.85358pts_{j}\!=\!\sum_{i=1}^{4}\!M_{\mathfrak{n}_{i}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Hence, from the second condition in Eq.(
40
),
we derive the following four-point mass spectral condition:

\sum_{i=1}^{4}\!M_{\mathfrak{n}_{i}}^{2}=\hskip 0.85358ptM^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}\!+M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}\!+M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(41)

where the left-hand side sums up the squared-mass
M_{\mathfrak{n}_{i}}^{2}

of each external KK state and the right-hand side is the sum
of the internal pole-mass-squared of all three kinematic channels. This condition requires that the sum of the squared-masses of
all the external KK states equals the sum of the
internal squared-mass-poles in the
(s,t,u)
channels. It should hold for any four-point scattering amplitudes
of the KK gauge bosons and of the KK gravitons
in the KK gauge/gravity theories
under the 5d toroidal
S^{1}
compactification (without orbifold).

For the 5d toroidal
S^{1}
compactification, the KK mass

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}_{i}}\!\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{i}/R\,
and the KK numbers are
conserved in each interaction vertex. Thus, the pole mass in each of the
(s,t,u)
channels
is given by

M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}\!=M_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}\!+\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}\!=M_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}\!+\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}\!=M_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}\!+\mathfrak{n}_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(42)

With these we can re-express the mass spectral condition
(
41
) as follows:

\mathfrak{n}_{1}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\mathfrak{n}_{2}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\mathfrak{n}_{3}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\mathfrak{n}_{4}^{2}\,=\,(\mathfrak{n}_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{2})^{2}\!+(\mathfrak{n}_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{4})^{2}\!+(\mathfrak{n}_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{3})^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(43)

This relation can be further reduced to

\mathfrak{n}_{1}\!+\mathfrak{n}_{2}\!+\mathfrak{n}_{3}\!+\mathfrak{n}_{4}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
,
which is just the same as the neutral condition (
33
)
and is thus guaranteed by the KK number conservation
under the toroidal compactification of
S^{1}
. The neutral condition (
33
) is again a direct outcome
of the conservation of KK indices under the 5d toroidal

S^{1}
compactification. Hence, the mass spectral condition (
41
) does hold
for the 5d toroidal
S^{1}
compactification.

Regarding the mass spectral condition (
41
) we have
some comments in order. First, we stress that Eq.(
41
) is both a

necessary and sufficient
condition
for
directly
realizing the massive double-copy construction
in any KK gauge/gravity theories under the 5d toroidal compactification. As we will explicitly demonstrate in Sec.
3.3
,
under the 5d compactification with
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold,
the condition (
41
) is violated for the four-point
KK scattering amplitudes and the direct double-copy is impossible. Instead we could realize the exact double-copy by decomposing each
KK graviton amplitude into a sum of the partial KK graviton amplitudes
which are constructed from the double-copy of the KK gauge boson
amplitudes under the
S^{1}
compactification

without

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}_{2}\!
orbifold. Second, for the gauge/gravity theories other than the
KK gauge/gravity theories under the toroidal compactification,
the BCJ-type double-copy could be realized even without obeying
the mass spectral condition (
41
). An important example is the double-copy construction
for the 3d topological Chern-Simons gauge/gravity theories
TMG
,
where we find that the BCJ-type color-kinematics duality and massive
double-copy can be realized
Hang:2021oso
, but the
spectral condition (
41
) is not obeyed
because all physical gauge bosons (gravitons) in the
pure non-Abelian Chern-Simons gauge (gravity) theories
have the same mass
M
and thus the condition (
41
)
becomes
\hskip 0.85358pt4M^{2}\!\neq\!3M^{2}
. Finally, we will further analyze the mass spectral condition
(
41
) in Section
5
and
demonstrate that without assuming an underlying theory
a priori, solving this spectral condition (
41
)
can uniquely determine the mass spectrum to be that
of the KK theories under the toroidal compactification.

In passing, from the KK gauge boson amplitude (
32
)
and the KK graviton amplitude (
39
), we can further
derive the double-copy formulation under the high energy expansion
of
\,s_{j}\!\gg\!M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}
. We expand the KK gauge boson amplitude (
32
) as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt\mathcal{T}\big[A_{\lambda_{1}}^{a\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\lambda_{2}}^{b\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\lambda_{3}}^{c\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{3}}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\lambda_{4}}^{d\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big]\hskip-0.85358pt

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358ptg^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\sum_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)\,}{\,s_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptM^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}\,}

\displaystyle=\hskip 1.42262ptg^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\sum_{j}\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{j}\big[\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)\!+\!\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)\big]\,}{\,s_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}\,}=\mathcal{T}_{0}+\delta\mathcal{T}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(44a)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)}{~1\!-M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}/s_{j}\,}

\displaystyle=\hskip 1.42262pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(44b)

Then, we can expand the double-copied four-point
KK graviton scattering amplitude (
39
) for

h_{\sigma_{1}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}h_{\sigma_{2}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\!\rightarrow\!h_{\sigma_{3}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}h_{\sigma_{4}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}

as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt}{16}\!\sum_{j}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\prod_{k=1}^{4}\!\hskip-1.42262ptC_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\!\frac{\,1\!-\!M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}\hskip-0.85358pt/\hskip-0.85358pts_{j}\,}{\,s_{\hskip-0.85358ptj}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda_{k})\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\delta\mathcal{N}^{\mathsf{P}}_{j}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda_{k})\Big]\!\Big[\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda_{k}^{\prime})\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\delta\mathcal{N}^{\mathsf{P}}_{j}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda_{k}^{\prime})\Big]

\displaystyle=\,\mathcal{M}_{0}+\delta\mathcal{M}\,,

(45)

where
\mathcal{M}_{0}
and
\delta\mathcal{M}
denote the expanded KK graviton amplitudes
at the leading order (LO) and next-to-leading order (NLO)
respectively,

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\mathcal{M}_{0}

\displaystyle\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\,16\,}\sum_{j}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\prod_{k}C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\frac{\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})~}{s_{j}}\,,

(46a)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\delta\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\,16\,}\!\sum_{j}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\!\prod_{k}\!\hskip-0.85358ptC_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\!\frac{\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})\!+\!\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\!-\!\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}/s_{j}\,}{s_{j}}.

(46b)

Using the general power counting method for the KK
theories
Hang:2021fmp
,
we can estimate that the above four-point LO
KK graviton amplitude
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}_{0}\!=\!O(E^{2}M_{n}^{0})\hskip 0.85358pt
and the
NLO KK graviton amplitude
\delta\mathcal{M}\!=\!O(E^{0}M_{n}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt
. The above expanded massive double-copy formulas
under the high energy expansion are
important for the formulation of the
KK gravitational equivalence theorem (GRET)
Hang:2021fmp

which quantitatively connects the scattering amplitudes
of the longitudinal KK gravitons to the scattering
amplitudes of the corresponding KK Goldstone bosons
in the high energy limit.

We derived the extended KLT relations
for the massive KK open/closed string amplitudes in Ref.
KKString

by using the compactified bosonic string theory. We note that this is closely related to the massive KK BCJ
construction as generally discussed in the previous section. For the four KK graviton scattering,
we use the massive KLT relation
KKString
to
express the tree-level KK graviton scattering amplitude
as the products of the two color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitudes:

\mathcal{M}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left[h_{\sigma_{1}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}h_{\sigma_{2}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}h_{\sigma_{3}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}h_{\sigma_{4}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\right]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{16}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\!\!\left(\prod_{k=1}^{4}\!\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\!\right)\!(t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{k}}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-0.85358pt[1234]\hskip 1.42262pt\mathcal{T}_{\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-0.85358pt[1324]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(47)

where the superscript
\mathsf{P}

on the right-hand-side denotes each given choice of
the KK indices of the external KK gravitons

\{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{2},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{3},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{4}\}
. For the case of elastic scattering, there are three types of
independent combinations of the KK numbers for the external state,

\{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{2},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{3},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{4}\}\!=\!\{\pm n,\pm n,\mp n,\mp n\},\{\pm n,\mp n,\mp n,\pm n\},\{\pm n,\mp n,\pm n,\mp n\}
. In the above Eq.(
47
),
we have used the shorthand notations
for the color-ordered amplitudes

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}]\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{k}}^{\mathsf{P}}[1234]\,
and

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}]\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{\lambda_{k}}^{\mathsf{P}}[1324]\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Then, we can express the above two color-ordered KK gauge boson
scattering amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\left(\!\!\begin{aligned} \mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-0.85358pt[1234]\\[2.84526pt]
-\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-0.85358pt[1324]\end{aligned}\right)\!

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\!-\frac{1}{\,s-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}^{2}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\,}~~~&-\frac{1}{t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}}\\[5.69054pt]
-\frac{1}{\,t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\,}\!~&-\frac{1}{\,t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,u-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}^{2}\,}\!\end{pmatrix}\!\!\!\left(\!\begin{aligned} \mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{P}}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{P}}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\!\equiv\Theta\!\left(\!\begin{aligned} \mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{P}}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{P}}\end{aligned}\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\!,

(48)

where we have used the notations

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}]\hskip-0.85358pt\!\equiv\!\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-0.85358pt[1234]\,
and

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}]\hskip-0.85358pt\!\equiv\!\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-0.85358pt[1324]\hskip 0.85358pt

with the helicity indices suppressed for simplicity. On the right-hand side of Eq.(
48
),
the kinematic numerators have encoded the helicity indices of
the external KK gauge bosons,

\mathcal{N}^{\mathsf{P}}_{s}\!=\!\mathcal{N}^{\mathsf{P}}_{s}(\lambda)
and

\mathcal{N}^{\mathsf{P}}_{u}\!=\!\mathcal{N}^{\mathsf{P}}_{u}(\lambda)
. In the derivation of Eq.(
48
),
we have used a massive fundamental BCJ identity:

(s-\!M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{P}}[1234]\,=\,(u-\!M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{P}}[1324]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(49)

which is deduced by applying the shifting method to the
conventional massless fundamental BCJ relation

\,s\hskip 1.42262pt\mathcal{T}[1234]\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358ptu\hskip 1.42262pt\mathcal{T}[1324]\hskip 0.85358pt

BCJ:2008

or by taking the field theory limit of the corresponding
relation for the KK open string amplitudes
KKString
. Then, we can substitute the color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitudes
in Eq.(
48
) into Eq.(
47
), and derive
the following BCJ-type KK graviton scattering amplitude:

\hskip-17.07164pt\mathcal{M}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left[h_{\sigma_{1}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}h_{\sigma_{2}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}h_{\sigma_{3}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}h_{\sigma_{4}}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\right]=\,-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{16}\!\sum_{j}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\!\!\left(\prod_{k=1}^{4}\!\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\!\right)\!\frac{~\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})~}{~s_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}^{2}~}\,.

(50)

The above fully agrees with the extended massive BCJ-type double-copy
formula (
39
) [which was derived from Eq.(
24
)
by using the shifting method]. This full agreement demonstrates that
the massive BCJ-type double-copy formula
(
50
) [or (
39
)] can be also
derived from the massive KK KLT relation (
47
)
(which was based on our previous derivation of the KK KLT relations
from analyzing the KK open/closed string amplitudes
KKString
). Hence, the two types of the massive double-copy constructions
of the four-point KK graviton amplitudes are shown
to be equivalent at tree level. We will systematically present the explicit results of the four-point
full scattering amplitudes of the massive longitudinal KK gravitons
in Appendix
C
.

Next, we further analyze the structure of the color-ordered
KK gauge boson amplitudes (
48
)
in connection to the massive fundamental BCJ relation
(
49
). This relation (
49
) shows that the two
color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitudes

\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{P}}[1234]
and
\mathcal{T}^{\mathsf{P}}[1324]
are not independent,
so the kernel matrix
\hskip 0.85358pt\Theta
of Eq.(
48
)
has rank
1\hskip 0.85358pt
and leads to a vanishing determinant

\det\Theta\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
. Using Eq.(
48
), we directly compute this determinant:

\det\Theta\,=\,-\frac{~s+t+u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}^{2})~}{(s\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}^{2})(t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2})(u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}^{2})}\,,

(51)

which should vanish once we impose a kinematic condition

\hskip-0.85358pts+t+u\hskip-0.85358pt=M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\!+M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt
.
Since Eq.(
201
) gives the sum of Mandelstam variables

\hskip 0.85358pts+t+u\!=\!\sum_{i=1}^{4}\!M_{\mathfrak{n}_{i}}^{2}
,
we can express this kinematic condition as follows:

\sum_{i=1}^{4}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}_{i}}^{2}=M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(52)

This just gives the same four-point mass spectral condition as
Eq.(
41
) which we derived earlier by requiring the
double-copied KK graviton scattering amplitude (
39
)
to be invariant under the generalized KK gauge transformation
(
34
). The above proof of the condition (
52
) relies on
using the massive KK fundamental BCJ relation (
49
)
for the rank reduction of the kernel matrix
\hskip 0.85358pt\Theta\hskip 0.85358pt
,
where we deduced Eq.(
49
) to hold for
the KK gauge theories under the 5d toroidal
S^{1}
compactification. The exact agreement between
the conditions (
41
) and (
52
)
means that the generalized KK gauge transformation
(
34
) and the massive KK fundamental BCJ relation
(
49
) are fully compatible.

In passing, we note that a recent literature
DC-4dx1
studied
the constraints on a massive double-copy between the massive YM theory and
the dRGT gravity model
dRGT
; they found that the massive kernel
matrix of the four-point bi-adjoint scalar amplitudes could take
the minimal rank if a spectral condition is met; their spectral condition
turns out to be similar to our above KK spectral condition
(
52
). But, the literature
dRGT
essentially differs from our
present study because we focus on the KK gauge/gravity theories
under the 5d toroidal compactification in which both the
massive KK fundamental BCJ relation (
49
)
and the massive KK double-copy construction
(
50
) or [(
39
)]
can be proven without additional assumption. Our mass spectral condition (
52
)
is a
prediction
of the KK gauge/gravity theories
under the 5d toroidal
S^{1}\hskip-0.85358pt
compactification. Moreover, our proof of the same condition (
41
)
only relies on the fact that the double-copied four-point KK graviton
scattering amplitudes (
39
)
should be gauge-invariant under the generalized
KK gauge transformation (
34
). This independently gives the simplest direct proof of
the spectral condition (
41
).

3.2
Explicit Double-Copy Construction of KK Graviton Amplitudes

In this subsection, we compute explicitly the four-point
elastic and inelastic scattering amplitudes
of the KK gauge bosons. Then, using the extended BCJ-type massive double-copy construction
of Section
3.1
,
we derive the corresponding four-point KK graviton
scattering amplitudes under the toroidal compactification of
S^{1}
.

For the convenience and simplicity of explicit demonstration of
our extended massive KK BCJ construction
of the four-point KK graviton amplitudes
in a compact analytical form, we will consider
the leading scattering amplitudes of the longitudinal KK gravitons,
which are defined by using an effective leading-order
polarization tensor

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\zeta}^{\mu\nu}_{L}\!\!=\!\zeta^{\mu}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pt\zeta^{\nu}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pt

of the KK graviton.
6
6

6

We will present the double-copied full scattering amplitudes of
longitudinal KK gravitons using the exact
longitudinal polarization tensor (
26b
)
in Appendix
C
.
Thus, we define the corresponding on-shell
longitudinal KK graviton field as follows:

\bar{h}_{L}=\bar{\zeta}^{\mu\nu}_{L}h_{\mu\nu}\,,\hskip 20.00003pt\bar{\zeta}^{\mu\nu}_{L}=\zeta^{\mu}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pt\zeta^{\nu}_{L}\,.

(53)

As we will demonstrate in this subsection, the effective longitudinal KK
graviton amplitudes defined by this effective leading-order
polarization tensor

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\zeta}^{\mu\nu}_{L}\!\!=\!\zeta^{\mu}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pt\zeta^{\nu}_{L}\hskip 0.85358pt

can always give the correct leading-order results of
the exact longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitudes
under the high energy expansion, and thus have a physical meaning.

\blacklozenge
Inelastic KK Scattering Amplitudes of

\,\{0,0,n,n
}:

Since the structures of the inelastic KK scattering amplitudes
are simpler than those of the elastic KK
scattering amplitudes, we will start with analyzing the
inelastic KK amplitudes. Thus, we first consider the simplest nontrivial example
of the inelastic scattering process. This is the “mixed” four-particle scattering process
of gauge bosons with the initial states being zero-mode
gauge bosons (gravitons) and the final states being
longitudinal KK gauge bosons (KK gravitons). The neutral condition (
33
) allows
two possible choices of the external KK states
under the
S^{1}
toroidal compactification:

\{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{2},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{3},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{4}\}=\{0,0,\pm n,\mp n\}\equiv\mathsf{A}\,,

(54)

where only one of the combinations gives the
independent amplitude. Then, we derive the following four-point gauge boson
scattering amplitude for this reaction:

\mathcal{T}\big[\hskip-0.85358ptA^{a\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{\pm 1}A^{b\hskip 0.85358pt0}_{\mp 1}A^{c\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}\hskip-0.85358ptA^{d\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}\big]=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{A}}\,}{s}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{A}}}{\,t-\!M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{A}}}{\,u-\!M_{n}^{2}\,}\!\right)\!,

(55)

where the kinematic numerators
\,\{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{A}}\}\,
are
given in Eq.(
204
) of Appendix
B
.
With this, we impose the CK duality and derive the
corresponding four-point scattering amplitude
of the longitudinal KK gravitons with the
external states chosen as in Eq.(
54
):

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\big[h_{\pm 2}^{0}h_{\mp 2}^{0}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\big]

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{16}\bigg[\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}\,}{s}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}\,}{\,t\!-\!M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}\,}{\,u\!-\!M_{n}^{2}~}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\,\frac{~\kappa^{2}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\!+\!4)^{2}\hskip 0.85358pts^{4}_{\theta}~}{~{8}\hskip 0.85358pt[(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-1.42262pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt]\,}\,,

(56)

where
\,\bar{s}\!=\!s/M_{n}^{2}\,
and

\,(s_{k\theta},\,c_{k\theta})\!\equiv\!(\sin\hskip-0.85358ptk\theta,\,\cos\hskip-0.85358ptk\theta)\hskip 0.85358pt
. In Eq.(
3.2
),
we have made the replacement of couplings

\,g^{2}\!\rightarrow\!-(\kappa/4)^{2}\,

based on Eq.(
23
). We see that the above double-copied inelastic graviton amplitude
with the leading-order longitudinal polarization tensor
(
53
) takes a fairly compact form. Then, we make the high energy expansion of the amplitude
(
3.2
) and derive the LO amplitude as follows:

\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[h_{\pm 2}^{0}h_{\mp 2}^{0}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{~\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pts^{2}_{\theta}\,s~}{\,{16}\,}=\frac{~\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pts~}{\,{32}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(57)

We further present the full graviton amplitude in
Eq.(
219
) of Appendix
C

by using the exact longitudinal polarization tensor
(
26b
) of the KK gravitons. Under the high energy expansion, the LO and NLO parts
of the full graviton amplitude (
219
) are
derived in Eqs.(
220a
) and (
220b
)
respectively and we have

{\mathcal{M}}\big[h_{\pm 2}^{0}h_{\mp 2}^{0}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\big]\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!{\mathcal{M}}_{0}+{\delta\mathcal{M}}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Comparing Eqs. (
57
) and (
220a
),
we can deduce:

\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[h_{\pm 2}^{0}h_{\mp 2}^{0}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\big]={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{\pm 2}^{0}h_{\mp 2}^{0}{h}_{L}^{\mp n}{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\big].

(58)

Hence, the scattering amplitude (
3.2
)
which we have computed using the leading polarization tensor
(
53
) of longitudinal KK gravitions
does have
an important physical meaning,
namely, its expanded LO amplitude
(
57
) can give the correct LO contribution
(
220a
)
of the full graviton amplitude (
219
). This is a striking feature because using the leading polarization tensor
(
53
) of longitudinal KK gravitions we only need to
compute the corresponding gauge boson amplitude including the external
longitudinal polarization state alone (for the double-copy
construction), but in the full graviton amplitude
its external longitudinal KK graviton state contains the exact
longitudinal polarization tensor
(
26b
)
which includes the products of the gauge boson polarization vectors
of both the helicities
\pm 1
and
0\hskip 0.85358pt
, and is thus much
more complicated. In the following, we will demonstrate explicitly that this
feature holds for all the four-point KK graviton scattering amplitudes
studied in this section.

\blacklozenge
Inelastic KK Scattering Amplitudes of

\{n,2n,3n,4n\}
:

As the second example, we study the inelastic KK scattering
process containing the following KK indices
of the external states:

\{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{2},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{3},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{4}\}=\{\pm n,\mp 2n,\mp 3n,\pm 4n\}\equiv\mathsf{A}\,.

(59)

Thus, we derive the following four-point leading
scattering amplitude of longitudinal KK gauge bosons:

\mathcal{T}\!\left[A^{a\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}A^{b\hskip 0.85358pt\mp 2n}_{L}A^{c\hskip 0.85358pt\mp 3n}_{L}A^{d\hskip 0.85358pt\pm 4n}_{L}\right]=\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{A}}}{\,s-M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{A}}}{\,t-25M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{A}}}{\,u-4M_{n}^{2}\,}\right)\!,

(60)

where the kinematic numerators
\,\{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{A}}\}\,

are given by Eq.(
204
). With the longitudinal KK gauge boson amplitude
(
60
) and using the CK duality, we compute
the corresponding four-point leading longitudinal KK
graviton amplitude as follows:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}^{\pm n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\mp 2n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\mp 3n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\pm 4n}_{L}\right]=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{16}\!\left[\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}}{~s\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}}{\,t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt25M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}}{\,u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}~}\right]\hskip 22.76219pt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}(49\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{\prime}\!-\!1)[(343\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{\prime 2}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt174\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{\prime}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt27)\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt12\hskip 0.85358pt{\omega}_{-}c_{\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt{\omega}_{+}^{2}c_{2\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt]^{2}~}{3136\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{\prime 2}\hskip 0.85358pt[(7\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{\prime}\!+\!3)\!+\hskip-0.85358pt{\omega}_{-}c_{\theta}][(7\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{\prime}\!-\!3)\!-\!{\omega}_{-}c_{\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt]}\,,\hskip 22.76219pt

(61)

where we have defined
\,\bar{s}\!=\!s/M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
,

\,\bar{s}^{\prime}\!=\!\bar{s}/49\hskip 0.85358pt
, and

\hskip 0.85358pt{\omega}_{\pm}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\hskip-0.85358pt\sqrt{49\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{\prime\hskip 0.85358pt2}\!\pm 58\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{\prime}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt9\,}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Then, making the high energy expansion for Eq.(
3.2
),
we derive the following LO KK graviton scattering amplitude:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}^{\pm n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\mp 2n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\mp 3n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\pm 4n}_{L}\big]=\frac{~\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pts~}{\,64\,}\hskip 0.85358pt(7\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})^{2}\csc^{2}\!\theta\,,\hskip 22.76219pt

(62)

which have
O(E^{2})
and are mass-independent, as expected.

Moreover, we have used the exact longitudinal polarization
tensor (
26b
) to further derive
the corresponding full inelastic KK graviton amplitudes

\mathcal{M}\!\left[{h}^{\pm n}_{L}{h}^{\mp 2n}_{L}{h}^{\mp 3n}_{L}{h}^{\pm 4n}_{L}\right]

in Eq.(
C
) of Appendix
C
. Under high energy expansion,
the LO and NLO contributions of the full amplitude
(
C
) are given by Eqs.(
223a
)
and (
223
), respectively. Inspecting the two LO amplitudes (
62
) and
(
223a
), we find that they are equal:

\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}^{\pm n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\mp 2n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\mp 3n}_{L}\bar{h}^{\pm 4n}_{L}\big]={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}^{\pm n}_{L}{h}^{\mp 2n}_{L}{h}^{\mp 3n}_{L}{h}^{\pm 4n}_{L}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(63)

Hence, as we observed earlier, the scattering amplitude (
3.2
)
which we have computed using the leading polarization tensor
(
53
) of longitudinal KK gravitions does have
an important physical meaning, because its expanded LO amplitude
(
62
) can give the correct LO contribution
(
223a
) of the full KK graviton amplitude (
C
).

\blacklozenge
Inelastic KK Scattering Amplitudes of

\{n,n,m,m\}
:

As the third example of the inelastic KK scattering process,
we study the four longitudinal KK gauge boson scattering
amplitude whose external states have the following KK indices:

\displaystyle\{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{2},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{3},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{4}\}\,=\,\left\{\begin{array}[]{r}\{\pm n,\mp n,\mp m,\pm m\}\equiv\mathsf{A}\,,\\[1.99168pt]
\{\pm n,\mp n,\pm m,\mp m\}\equiv\mathsf{B}\,,\end{array}\right.

(64)

where the positive integers
(n,\hskip 0.85358ptm)
are unequal
(
\hskip 0.85358ptn\!\neq\!m\hskip 0.85358pt
), and the inelastic amplitudes
of type-
\mathsf{A}
and type-
\mathsf{B}
are independent. Thus, we compute the corresponding leading longitudinal
KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\!\left[A^{a\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}A^{b\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}A^{c\hskip 0.85358pt\mp m}_{L}A^{\hskip 0.85358pt\pm m}_{L}\right]

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{A}}\,}{s}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{A}}\,}{t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n+m}^{2}}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{A}}\,}{u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n-m}^{2}}\right)\!,

(65a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\!\left[A^{a\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}A^{b\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}A^{c\hskip 0.85358pt\pm m}_{L}A^{d\hskip 0.85358pt\mp m}_{L}\right]

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\!\left(\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{B}}\,}{s}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{B}}}{\,t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n-m}^{2}\,}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{B}}}{\,u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n+m}^{2}\,}\right)\!,

(65b)

where
the kinematic numerators

\{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{A}},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{B}}\}
are presented
in Eqs.(
208
) and (
211
)
of Appendix
B
.

Using the inelastic KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes
(
65a
)-(
65b
)
and the extended massive CK duality,
we can derive the following leading scattering amplitudes
for longitudinal KK gravitons:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\right]=-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\,16\,}\bigg[\,\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}}{s}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}}{\,t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n+m}^{2}\,}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}}{\,u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n-m}^{2}\,}\,\bigg]\hskip 34.1433pt

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt[(7\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!12\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358ptr_{+}^{2}\!+\!48\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2})\!+\!64\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}^{\prime}rc_{\theta}\!+\!(\bar{s}\!+\!4)(\bar{s}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2})c_{2\theta}]^{2}~}{64\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\!-\!4\hskip 0.85358ptr\!-\!4\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}^{\prime}c_{\theta})(\bar{s}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358ptr\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}^{\prime}c_{\theta})}\,,

(66a)

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\right]=-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\,16\,}\bigg[\,\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{B}})^{2}}{s}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{B}})^{2}}{\,t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n-m}^{2}\,}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{B}})^{2}}{\,u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n+m}^{2}\,}\,\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt[(7\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!12\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358ptr_{+}^{2}\!+\!48\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2})\!-\!64\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}^{\prime}rc_{\theta}\!+\!(\bar{s}\!+\!4)(\bar{s}\!+\!4r^{2})c_{2\theta}]^{2}~}{64\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\!-\!4\hskip 0.85358ptr\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}^{\prime}c_{\theta})(\bar{s}\!+\!4\hskip 0.85358ptr\!-\!4\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{q}^{\prime}c_{\theta})}\,,

(66b)

where we have used the notations,

\displaystyle r=\hskip-0.85358ptM_{m}/M_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 11.38109ptr_{+}^{2}=1+r^{2},\hskip 11.38109ptq\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\sqrt{E^{2}\!-\!M_{n}^{2}\,}\,,\hskip 11.38109ptq^{\prime}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\sqrt{E^{2}\!-\!M_{m}^{2}\,}\,,

\displaystyle\bar{q}^{2}\!=q^{2}/M_{n}^{2}=\bar{s}/4\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt1\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 8.53581pt\bar{q}^{\prime 2}\!=q^{\prime 2}\hskip-0.85358pt/M_{n}^{2}=\bar{s}/4\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptr^{2},\hskip 8.53581pt\bar{q}^{2}\bar{q}^{\prime 2}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358ptr^{2})/16\hskip 0.85358pt.~~~\hskip 8.53581pt

Making the high energy expansion, we can derive the LO amplitudes
of Eqs.(
66
)-(
66
) as follows:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\big]=\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\big]=\frac{~\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pts~}{\,64\,}\hskip 0.85358pt(7\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})^{2}\csc^{2}\!\theta\,,

(68)

which are of
O(E^{2})
and mass-independent, as expected.
We note that the LO amplitudes (
62
) and
(
68
) coincide.

In addition, using the exact longitudinal polarization tensor
(
26b
) for each external KK graviton state,
we have further derived the full inelastic KK graviton
scattering amplitudes

\mathcal{M}\!\left[{h}_{L}^{\pm n}{h}_{L}^{\mp n}{h}_{L}^{\mp m}{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\right]

and

\mathcal{M}\!\left[{h}_{L}^{\pm n}{h}_{L}^{\mp n}{h}_{L}^{\pm m}{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\right]

in Eqs.(
224
)-(
225
)
of Appendix

. Under the high energy expansion, their LO and NLO scattering

) with

we find that they are equal:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\big]=\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\big]

\displaystyle=\,\,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}_{L}^{\pm n}{h}_{L}^{\mp n}{h}_{L}^{\mp m}{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\big]={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}_{L}^{\pm n}{h}_{L}^{\mp n}{h}_{L}^{\pm m}{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\big].

(69)

This is similar to Eqs.(
58
) and (
63
),
which we have demonstrated for other four-point KK graviton

\blacklozenge
Elastic KK Scattering Amplitudes of

\{n,n,n,n\}
:

Next, we study the elastic scattering processes for the
four longitudinal KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons. The external states are allowed to have the following combinations
of KK indices:

\displaystyle\{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{2},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{3},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{4}\}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{r}\{\pm n,\pm n,\mp n,\mp n\}\equiv\mathsf{A}\,,\\[2.27621pt]
\{\pm n,\mp n,\mp n,\pm n\}\equiv\mathsf{B}\,,\\[2.27621pt]
\{\pm n,\mp n,\pm n,\mp n\}\equiv\mathsf{C}\,,\end{array}\right.

(70)

where only three types of them, denoted by
(\mathsf{A},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{B},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{C})
,
are independent. Then, we compute the four-point elastic scattering amplitudes
of longitudinal KK gauge bosons as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\!\left[A^{a\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}A^{b\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}A^{c\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}A^{d\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}\right]

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{A}}}{\,s\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{A}}\,}{t}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{A}}\,}{u}\!\right)\!,

(71a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\!\left[A^{a\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}A^{b\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}A^{c\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}A^{d\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}\right]

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{B}}\,}{s}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{B}}}{\,t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{B}}\,}{u}\!\right)\!,

(71b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\!\left[A^{a\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}A^{b\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}A^{c\hskip 0.85358pt\pm n}_{L}A^{d\hskip 0.85358pt\mp n}_{L}\right]

\displaystyle=\,g^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{C}}\,}{s}+\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{C}}\,}{t}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{C}}}{\,u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}\,}\!\right)\!,

(71c)

where the kinematic numerators

\{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{A}},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{B}},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{C}}\}

are given by Eqs.(
213
)-(
217
)
of Appendix
B
.

Using the above KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes
(
71a
)-(
71c
)
and the extended massive CK duality,
we derive the following leading elastic scattering amplitudes
of longitudinal KK gravitons:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\big]=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{16}\bigg[\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}}{\,s\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}\,}{t}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{A}})^{2}\,}{u}\bigg],\hskip 14.22636pt

(72a)

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\right]=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{16}\bigg[\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{B}})^{2}\,}{s}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{B}})^{2}}{\,t\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{B}})^{2}\,}{u}\bigg],\hskip 14.22636pt

(72b)

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\right]=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{16}\bigg[\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{s}^{\mathsf{C}})^{2}\,}{s}+\frac{\,(\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\mathsf{C}})^{2}\,}{t}+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{u}^{\mathsf{C}})^{2}}{\,u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}~}\bigg].\hskip 14.22636pt

(72c)

After substituting the expressions of the numerators
into the above formulas, we derive the explicit forms
of these scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}~}{64}(\bar{s}\!-\!4)(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\csc^{2}\hskip-1.42262pt\theta\,,

(73a)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt[(7\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!24\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\!+\!48)\!+\!16\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)c_{\theta}\!+\!(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)^{2}c_{2\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt]^{2}\csc^{2}\!\frac{{\theta}}{2}~}{128\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\!-\!4)[(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)c_{\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt]}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(73b)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt[(7\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!24\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt48)\!-\!16\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)c_{\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)^{2}c_{2\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt]^{2}\sec^{2}\!\frac{{\theta}}{2}~}{128\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)[(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)c_{\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt]}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(73c)

Then, making the high energy expansion, we derive the following
LO scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\big]=\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\big]=\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pts~}{64}(7\!+\!c_{2\theta})^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\hskip-1.42262pt\theta\,.

(74)

We see that the LO elastic KK graviton amplitudes
of the above three helicity combinations
are equal and are mass-independent, as expected.

Moreover, using the exact longitudinal polarization tensor
(
26b
) for each external KK graviton state,
we have further derived the full elastic KK graviton
scattering amplitudes
in Eqs.(
230
)-(
231
)
of Appendix
C
. The LO and NLO contributions of these KK graviton scattering amplitudes
are given in Eq.(
230
). Comparing Eqs.(
3.2
) and (
232a
),
we find that they are equal:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\big]=\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\big]=\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\big]\hskip 34.1433pt

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358pt\,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\mp n}\big]={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\pm n}\big]={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}\big].

(75)

In general, we expect that the equality between the two types of the
LO KK graviton amplitudes, such as those shown in
Eqs.(
58
), (
63
), (
69
), and (
75
),
can hold for any such
N
-point KK graviton
scattering amplitudes at the LO (with some or all external KK graviton
states being longitudinally polarized), as long as
these KK amplitudes respect the
KK GAET
5DYM2002
KK-ET-He2004

and KK GRET
Hang:2021fmp

in which the residual terms can be supressed and belong to the NLO. For the current study, considering the four-point KK
scattering amplitudes, we can use the KK GAET and GRET to prove
this equality. For any four longitudinal KK gauge boson scattering
amplitude, the KK GAET states
5DYM2002
KK-ET-He2004

that under the high energy expansion its LO scattering amplitude
of longitudinal KK gauge bosons (
A_{L}^{a\mathfrak{n}}
)
equals that of the corresponding
KK Goldstone bosons (
A_{5}^{a\mathfrak{n}}
) at the LO:

\mathcal{T}_{0}\big[A_{L}^{a\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\!A_{L}^{b\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\!A_{L}^{c\mathfrak{n}_{3}}\!A_{L}^{d\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big]=\mathcal{T}_{0}\big[A_{5}^{a\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\!A_{5}^{b\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\!A_{5}^{c\mathfrak{n}_{3}}\!A_{5}^{d\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big],

(76)

which are of
O(g^{2}\hskip-0.85358ptE^{0})
and
are mass-independent.
7
7

7

This KK GAET
5DYM2002
KK-ET-He2004
formulates the
geometric “Higgs” mechanism for the KK gauge boson mass-generation
at the scattering
S
-matrix level. It differs from the usual 4d equivalence theorem (ET)
SM-ET

of the SM (describing the conventional Higgs mechanism
at the
S
-matrix level) because the geometric KK mass-generation
and the KK GAET do not invoke any Higgs boson.
As we have demonstrated in this work and
Hang:2021fmp
,
we can make double-copy construction on both sides of
Eq.(
76
) and thus deduce the corresponding
GRET formula which connects the LO scattering amplitude
of longitudinal KK gravitons (
\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}}
)
to that of the corresponding
KK Goldstone bosons (
h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}}
) at the LO:

\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big]=\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big],

(77)

where on the left-hand-side of the equality each external
longitudinal KK graviton state

\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}}\!=\bar{\zeta}_{\mu\nu}h_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mu\nu}

has its leading-order longitudinal polarization tensor defined
in Eq.(
53
).
8
8

8

We note that the double-copy of the LO scattering amplitudes
of the KK Goldstone bosons,
\,\mathcal{T}_{0}\big[A_{5}^{a\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\!A_{5}^{b\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\!A_{5}^{c\mathfrak{n}_{3}}\!A_{5}^{d\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big]\!\!\rightarrow\!\!\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big]
,
is well justified because at the LO of the high energy expansion
the KK Goldstone bosons
A_{5}^{a\mathfrak{n}}
and
h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}}

behave as massless states and their LO amplitudes are
mass-independent
Hang:2021fmp
. Hence, the double-copy
of the LO KK Goldstone amplitudes should be guaranteed by the
double-copy of the corresponding massless scattering amplitudes,

\mathcal{T}\big[\hat{A}_{5}^{a}\hat{A}_{5}^{b}\hat{A}_{5}^{c}\hat{A}_{5}^{d}\big]\!\!\rightarrow\!\mathcal{M}\big[\hat{h}_{55}\hat{h}_{55}\hat{h}_{55}\hat{h}_{55}\big]
,
in the non-compactified 5d gauge/gravity theories. This conclusion also holds for the general
N
-point massless
Goldstone boson amplitudes as is obvious.
On the other hand, the KK GRET
Hang:2021fmp

gives the following LO equality:

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big]=\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big],

(78)

where according to the original
GRET formulation
Hang:2021fmp
,
the LO scattering amplitude of longitudinal KK gravitons
on the left-hand-side of the equality is computed
by using the exact longitudinal polarization tensor
(
26b
) for each external KK graviton state

\hskip 0.85358pt{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}}\!={\zeta}_{\mu\nu}h_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mu\nu}
. Comparing Eqs.(
77
) and (
78
),
we deduce the following identity for the two types of
four-point longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitudes at the LO:

\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big]={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}\big].

(79)

We can extend the above identity to the general case of the
scattering amplitudes of
N
longitudinal KK gravitons
(
N\!\geqslant\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt
):

\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\cdots\bar{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{N}}\big]={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\cdots{h}_{L}^{\mathfrak{n}_{N}}\big].

(80)

We can readily prove the
N
-point identity (
80
)
by repeating the same reasoning of the above proof for the four-point
scattering case, because the GRET generally holds
for
N
longitudinal KK graviton
scattering amplitudes
Hang:2021fmp

and the double-copy also holds for

N
-point massless scalar KK Goldstone boson amplitudes
(as noticed in footnote-5). We have explicitly verified this identity for a number of
typical four-point longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitudes
as shown in Eqs.(
63
),
(
69
), and (
75
). We can readily extend the above proof to other four-point
KK graviton scattering amplitudes
and to the case of
N
-point
KK graviton scattering amplitudes with
N\!\!>\!4\hskip 1.42262pt
. For the extension to other cases, the scattering amplitudes
should contain more than one external longitudinal KK state. This is because
for a given scattering amplitude having only one
external longitudinal KK state, the the residual
term of the KK GAET or GRET has the same order as the
LO
A_{5}^{\mathfrak{n}}
amplitude or the LO

h_{55}^{\mathfrak{n}}
amplitude
Hang:2021fmp
; hence
the LO amplitudes of KK longitudinal gauge bosons and
of KK Goldstone bosons will not equal,
unlike the case of Eq.(
76
)
or Eq.(
78
). This means that the extension
of our conclusion of Eq.(
79
)
or Eq.(
80
) to
other KK graviton scattering amplitudes should contain
more than one external longitudinal KK state. In addition, we note that
the identities (
79
)-(
80
)
and their extension should hold for any consistent
5d KK compactifications including the
S^{1}
compactification
and the orbifold compactification of
S^{1}\hskip-0.85358pt\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

According to the LO double-copy identities
(
46
) and (
80
),
we can translate the identity (
79
) into
a nontrivial sum rule condition on the LO kinematic numerators
of KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes:

\sum_{j}\!\frac{1}{\,s_{j}\,}\!\left\{\!\Big[\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(L)\Big]^{2}\!-\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\prod_{k}\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\,\sigma_{\!k}}\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})}\!\right\}=\,0\,.

(81)

In summary, the above identities (
79
) and (
80
) demonstrate that
under high energy expansion,
each leading-order (LO) scattering amplitude of longitudinal
KK gravitions computed by using the simple effective leading
longitudinal polarization tensor (
53
) always
equals the LO amplitude computed by using the exact longitudinal polarization tensor
(
25
)-(
26
). This leads to the above nontrivial sum rule condition
(
81
),
from which we draw an important conclusion: each
N
-point
longitudinal KK graviton scattering amplitude (with two or more
external KK graviton states being longitudinally polarized) can be
constructed by double-copy of a single amplitude of KK gauge bosons
(in which the corresponding KK gauge bosons are longitudinally
polarized only) according to the effective leading
longitudinal polarization tensor (
53
).

3.3
KK Amplitudes and Double-Copy under
Orbifold Compactification

In this subsection, we further study the massive double-copy
construction of the four-point scattering amplitudes of
KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons,
under the 5d orbifold compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. In this case, the KK gauge boson fields
A^{a\mu}_{n}

and KK graviton fields
h^{\mu\nu}_{n}

are defined as
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
even
Hang:2021fmp
. Thus, the KK amplitudes can be expressed
as the sum of the relevant subamplitudes under the
S^{1}

toroidal compactification without orbifold. Similar to Eq.(
17
),
the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
even states of KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons
can be defined as follows:

\displaystyle A_{\lambda}^{a\hskip 0.85358ptn}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\!\left[A_{\lambda}^{a(+n)}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\lambda}^{a(-n)}\right]\!,~~~~h_{\sigma}^{n}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,}\,}\!\left[h_{\sigma}^{(+n)}\!+\hskip-0.85358pth_{\sigma}^{(-n)}\right]\!,

(82)

where we denote the helicity of KK gauge boson by

\hskip 0.85358pt\lambda\,(=\!0,\pm 1)\hskip 0.85358pt
and the helicity of KK graviton by

\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma\,(=\!0,\pm 1,\pm 2)\hskip 0.85358pt
. With these, we can derive the four-point scattering amplitudes
of the KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}[A_{\lambda_{1}}^{an_{1}}\!A_{\lambda_{2}}^{bn_{2}}\!A_{\lambda_{3}}^{cn_{3}}\!A_{\lambda_{4}}^{dn_{4}}]

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{~g^{2}~}{\,2^{\scriptsize{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$N$}}}\!/2\,}}\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\sum_{j}\,\frac{~\mathcal{C}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}(\lambda)~}{~s_{j}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptM^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}~}\,,

(83a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}[h_{\sigma_{1}}^{n_{1}}\hskip 0.85358pth_{\sigma_{2}}^{n_{2}}\hskip 0.85358pth_{\sigma_{3}}^{n_{3}}\hskip 0.85358pth_{\sigma_{4}}^{n_{4}}]

\displaystyle\,=\,-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{\,2^{\scriptsize{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$N$}}}\hskip-0.85358pt/2+4}\,}\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\sum_{j}\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\prod_{k=1}^{4}\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\frac{~\mathcal{N}^{\mathsf{P}}_{j}(\lambda_{k})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{P}}_{j}(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})~}{s_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}}\,,\hskip 19.91692pt

(83b)

where the external KK indices are non-negative integers

\hskip 0.85358ptn_{i}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and the sum of
\mathsf{P}
runs over the possible combinations
of the external KK indices
which obey the neutral condition (
33
). In the above formulas, the external states could contain possible
zero-mode gauge bosons (gravitons), so we denote the number of
external KK states (with
n_{i}\!>\hskip-0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt
) as

\overline{\hbox{}}
N
,
and thus the number of the external zero-mode states
(with
n_{i}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt
) equals
(4\hskip-0.85358pt-\!\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.94992pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$N$}})
. The factor
\,2^{-\scriptsize{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$N$}}}/2}

arises from the overall coefficient
\hskip 0.85358pt1/\!\sqrt{2}\,

of Eq.(
82
).
Substituting the kinematic numerators into
Eqs.(
83a
)-(
83b
),
we have verified that the scattering amplitudes
(
83a
)-(
83b
) agree with
our previous results
KKString
.

In addition, using the method of
Eqs.(
3.1
)-(
46
),
we can make high energy expansion for the double-copied KK
graviton amplitides (
83b
). Similar to Eqs.(
3.1
)-(
46
),
we derive the LO and NLO scattering amplitudes of four
KK gravitons from Eq.(
83b
),

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{0}+\delta\mathcal{M}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
under the
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
orbifold compactification:

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\mathcal{M}_{0}

\displaystyle\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{\,2^{\scriptsize{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$N$}}}\hskip-0.85358pt/2+4}\,}\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\sum_{j}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\prod_{k}\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\frac{~\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})~}{s_{j}}\,,

(84a)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\delta\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{\,2^{\scriptsize{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$N$}}}\hskip-0.85358pt/2+4}\,}\!\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\mathsf{P},\hskip 0.85358ptj}\!\!\prod_{k}\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\!\frac{~\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})\!+\!\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\!-\!N_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})N_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}/s_{j}\,}{s_{j}},

(84b)

where the LO and NLO kinematic numerators

(\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!,\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}})

are derived under the high energy expansion,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)}{~1\!-M^{2}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}/s_{j}\,}

\displaystyle=\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)\hskip-0.85358pt+\delta\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-1.42262pt(\lambda)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(85)

For the four-point elastic KK graviton scattering,
we verified
Hang:2021fmp
explicitly
that the above BCJ-type massive double-copy
formulas give the correct results at both the LO and NLO,
and they agree with the results of the extended massive KLT
relations
KKString

derived from the KK string theory approach.

Using Eq.(
82
) and (
83a
),
we can derive relations between

the
\hskip 0.85358ptS^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
orbifold compactification
and the corresponding KK scattering amplitudes under
the
S^{1}
compactification. For the inelastic KK scattering processes

we derive the following relations:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}\!\left[{h}_{\pm 2}^{0}{h}_{\mp 2}^{0}{h}_{L}^{n}{h}_{L}^{n}\right]

\displaystyle=\,{\mathcal{M}}\!\left[{h}_{\pm 2}^{0}{h}_{\mp 2}^{0}{h}_{L}^{\pm n}{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\right]\!,

(86a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}\!\left[{h}^{n}_{L}{h}^{2n}_{L}{h}^{3n}_{L}{h}^{4n}_{L}\right]

\displaystyle=\,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\,2\,}$}{\mathcal{M}}\!\left[{h}^{\pm n}_{L}{h}^{\mp 2n}_{L}{h}^{\mp 3n}_{L}{h}^{\pm 4n}_{L}\right]\!.

The above relations hold for the external longitudinal KK graviton

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\zeta}_{L}^{\mu\nu}

we have used the fact of

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{M}\big[{h}_{\pm 2}^{0}{h}_{\mp 2}^{0}{h}_{L}^{\pm n}{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\big]\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt

based upon Eq.(
54
),
whereas for deriving the relation (
86b
),
we have used the fact that Eq.(
59
) gives
all the allowed combinations of the external KK indices in
this scattering process.

For the inelastic KK scattering process

(n,n)\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!(m,m)
discussed in the example-III
of Section
3.2

under
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
compactification:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\bar{h}_{L}^{m}\bar{h}_{L}^{m}\right]=\,\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\,2\,}$}\hskip-0.85358pt\left\{\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\right]\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}\!\left[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm m}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp m}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=429\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt10120\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt26976\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt36352\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt19200\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}+34816\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle X_{4}

\displaystyle=2\hskip 0.85358pt(39\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt280\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!+32\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt11264\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt20224\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}-\hskip-0.85358pt2048)\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle X_{6}

\displaystyle=3\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+8\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!+160\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!-512\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt1280\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}-\hskip-0.85358pt2048\hskip 0.85358pt.

Then, making high energy expansion for the amplitude
(
90
) and using Eq.(
3.2
),
we derive the following LO elastic KK graviton amplitude
of Eq.(
90
):

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\big]=\frac{{\,3\,}}{2}\,\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\pm n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\bar{h}_{L}^{\mp n}\big]=\frac{~3\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa^{2}\hskip 0.85358pts~}{128}(7\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\hskip-1.42262pt\theta\,.

(92)

This equals the LO elastic KK graviton amplitude
(
235a
) [as derived by using the full
elastic KK graviton amplitude (
C
)],

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\bar{h}_{L}^{n}\big]={\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}_{L}^{n}{h}_{L}^{n}{h}_{L}^{n}{h}_{L}^{n}\big].

(93)

This agrees with the identity (
79
). We note that based upon the first equality of
Eq.(
92
) together with
Eq.(
93
),
we can make a double-copy construction
using directly the KK gauge boson scattering amplitude

\mathcal{T}_{0}\big[A_{L}^{an_{1}}\!A_{L}^{bn_{2}}\!A_{L}^{cn_{3}}\!A_{L}^{dn_{4}}\big]

under
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
as in
Hang:2021fmp

and deduce the correct LO KK graviton scattering amplitude

{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}_{L}^{n}{h}_{L}^{n}{h}_{L}^{n}{h}_{L}^{n}\big]

as in Eq.(
235a
).

In fact the identity (
79
) generally
holds for the current KK compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

as well. According to the LO double-copy formula (
84a
),
we can express the LO KK graviton scattering amplitudes
on the two sides of Eq.(
79
) as follows:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{M}$}}_{0}\big[\bar{h}_{L}^{n_{1}}\bar{h}_{L}^{n_{2}}\bar{h}_{L}^{n_{3}}\bar{h}_{L}^{n_{4}}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{\,64\,}\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\sum_{j}\!\frac{1}{\,s_{j}\,}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left[\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(L)\right]^{\hskip-0.85358pt2}\!,

(94a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[{h}_{L}^{n_{1}}{h}_{L}^{n_{2}}{h}_{L}^{n_{3}}{h}_{L}^{n_{4}}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}~}{\,64\,}\!\!\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\sum_{j}\!\frac{1}{\,s_{j}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\bigg[\hskip-0.85358pt\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\!\prod_{k}\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})}\bigg],

(94b)

Thus, we can translate the identity (
79
) into
a spectral condition on the LO kinematic numerators
of KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes:

\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\sum_{j}\!\frac{1}{\,s_{j}\,}\!\bigg\{\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(L)\Big]^{\hskip-0.85358pt2}\!-\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\prod_{k}\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda_{k})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}_{j}^{0,\mathsf{P}}\!(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})}\hskip-0.85358pt\bigg\}=\,0\,.

(95)

We see that this condition is guaranteed by the stronger
condition (
81
),
because Eq.(
81
) holds for each
given combination “
\mathsf{P}
” of KK indices of external states
and has no extra summation over all allowed combinations
“
\mathsf{P}
”.

3.4
Five-Point Massive KK Graviton
Amplitudes from Double-Copy

Figure 1:
Independent structures of the color-dressed
five-point scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons
or KK gravitons at tree level,
where the numbers
\{1,\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,5\}
denote
the KK indices of the external KK states,

\{1,\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,5\}\!\sim\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\mathfrak{n}_{5}\}
, and each internal line
has its KK index
\mathfrak{n}_{ij}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{i}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

The above analyses of the four-point KK scattering amplitudes
can be further extended to the
N
-point scattering amplitudes
with
\hskip 0.85358ptN\!\geqslant\hskip-0.85358pt5\hskip 1.42262pt
.
In this subsection, we study the double-copy construction of
the five-point massive KK graviton scattering amplitudes
with external states having KK indices

(\mathfrak{n}_{1},\mathfrak{n}_{2},\mathfrak{n}_{3},\mathfrak{n}_{4},\mathfrak{n}_{5})
.

There are 15 independent structures of the five-point
scattering amplitudes of the KK gauge bosons or KK gravitons,
and each structure contains two kinematic poles in total,
which we present in Fig.
1
. Thus, using the extended massive BCJ-type double-copy formula
(
24
) of the
N
-point KK graviton amplitudes,
we can derive the structure of the general five-point
scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons
under the
S^{1}
compactification. For each given combination of KK indices

\{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\cdots\!,\mathfrak{n}_{5}\}
of external states,
we can construct the KK graviton scattering amplitude as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\big[h_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}h_{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}^{\sigma_{2}}h_{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}^{\sigma_{3}}h_{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}^{\sigma_{4}}h_{\mathfrak{n}_{5}}^{\sigma_{5}}\big]=\frac{~\kappa^{3}\,}{64}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\prod_{k=1}^{5}\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}\Bigg[\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{1}}{(s_{12}\!-\!M_{12}^{2})(s_{34}\!-\!M_{34}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{2}}{(s_{12}\!-\!M_{12}^{2})(s_{35}\!-\!M_{35}^{2})}\hskip 17.07164pt

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 22.76219pt+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{3}}{(s_{12}\!-\!M_{12}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{4}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{4}}{(s_{13}\!-\!M_{13}^{2})(s_{24}-M_{24}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{5}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{5}}{(s_{13}\!-\!M_{13}^{2})(s_{25}-M_{25}^{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 22.76219pt+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{6}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{6}}{(s_{13}\!-\!M_{13}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{7}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{7}}{(s_{14}\!-\!M_{14}^{2})(s_{23}-M_{23}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{8}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{8}}{(s_{14}\!-\!M_{14}^{2})(s_{25}-M_{25}^{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 22.76219pt+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{9}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{9}}{(s_{14}\!-\!M_{14}^{2})(s_{35}\!-\!M_{35}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{10}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{10}}{(s_{15}\!-\!M_{15}^{2})(s_{23}-M_{23}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{11}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{11}}{(s_{15}\!-\!M_{15}^{2})(s_{24}-M_{24}^{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 22.76219pt+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{12}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{12}}{(s_{15}\!-\!M_{15}^{2})(s_{34}\!-\!M_{34}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{13}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{13}}{(s_{23}-M_{23}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{14}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{14}}{(s_{24}-M_{24}^{2})(s_{35}\!-\!M_{35}^{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 22.76219pt+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{15}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{15}}{(s_{25}-M_{25}^{2})(s_{34}\!-\!M_{34}^{2})}\Bigg].

(96)

In the above amplitude,
for simplicity we have used the shorthand notations
of the shifted massive numerators which are given by

\{\mathcal{N}_{j},\,\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\prime}\}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\{\mathcal{N}_{j}(\lambda_{k}),\,\mathcal{N}_{j}(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})\}\Big|_{s_{j}\rightarrow\hskip 1.42262pts_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}^{2}},

(97)

where we also make the replacements for products of
the external-state polarizations and momenta

\hat{\zeta}_{i}\!\cdot\hat{p}_{j}\!=\zeta_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358ptp_{j}
and

\hat{\zeta}_{i}\cdot\hat{\zeta}_{j}=\zeta_{i}\cdot\zeta_{j}\,

according to Eq.(
12
). On the right-hand side of Eq.(
97
),
the kinematic numerators

\{\mathcal{N}_{j}(\lambda_{k}),\,\mathcal{N}_{j}(\lambda^{\prime}_{k})\}
are given by
the corresponding five-point massless gauge boson amplitudes. The kinematic numerators of the five-point massless gauge boson amplitudes were studied in many literatures
BCJ:2019
and
can be obtained by a number of methods, such as the direct Feynman
diagram calculation, the CHY formula, and the low energy limit
of open string amplitudes. To save space we will not list all
the explicit formulas of these five-point massless numerators. As an example, we consider a sample five-point scattering amplitude
of the inelastic process
(2n,n)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n,n)
, where
all the external KK states are chosen to be
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip-1.42262pt
even. Thus, given the condition of the KK number conservation

\sum_{i=1}^{5}\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{i}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
for each KK sub-amplitude,
we find the following allowed sub-amplitudes with their external
KK gauge bosons (gravitons) having the KK-numbers:

\displaystyle\{+2n,+n,-n,-n,-n\}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 7.11317pt\{+2n,-n,+n,-n,-n\}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 7.11317pt\{+2n,-n,-n,+n,-n\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle\{+2n,-n,-n,-n,+n\}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 7.11317pt\{\mathrm{all\ permutations\ of\ \left(+,-\right)}\},

(98)

where for convenience we assign the external state-1 to have
the KK number
+2n\hskip 0.85358pt
. For instance, we can explicitly
construct the KK longitudinal graviton scattering amplitude
with the external KK-number combination
\{+2n,+n,-n,-n,-n\}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\big[h^{+2n}_{L}h^{+n}_{L}h^{-n}_{L}h^{-n}_{L}h^{-n}_{L}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{~\kappa^{3}\,}{64}\!\sum_{\lambda_{k},\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}\!\prod_{k=1}^{5}\!C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt0}\Bigg[\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{1}}{\,(s_{12}\!-\!9M_{n}^{2})(s_{34}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})\,}

\displaystyle~+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{2}}{(s_{12}\!-\!9M_{n}^{2})(s_{35}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{3}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{3}}{(s_{12}\!-\!9M_{n}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{4}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{4}}{(s_{13}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})s_{24}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{5}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{5}}{(s_{13}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})s_{25}}

\displaystyle~+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{6}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{6}}{(s_{13}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{7}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{7}}{(s_{14}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})s_{23}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{8}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{8}}{(s_{14}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})s_{25}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{9}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{9}}{(s_{14}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})(s_{35}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})}

\displaystyle~+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{10}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{10}}{(s_{15}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})s_{23}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{11}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{11}}{(s_{15}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})s_{24}}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{12}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{12}}{(s_{15}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})(s_{34}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{13}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{13}}{s_{23}(s_{45}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})}

\displaystyle~+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{14}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{14}}{s_{24}(s_{35}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{15}\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{15}}{s_{25}(s_{34}\!-\!4M_{n}^{2})}\Bigg]\,.

(99)

The five-point KK graviton scattering amplitudes with other
combinations of the external KK indices given in Eq.(
3.4
) can be constructed in the
similar manner.

3.5
Nonrelativistic Scattering Amplitudes
of KK States

The KK compactification predicts an infinite tower of KK states
for each type of particles
in the compactified 4d effective theory, which serve as the
KK excitation states of the corresponding zero-mode state. The KK mass spectrum always contains many heavy KK states,
so they can have KK masses much larger than
their kinetic energy and thus become nonrelativistic. Hence it is interesting to study how such nonrelativistic
scattering amplitudes of heavy KK states behave. In addition, there are recent studies to extract the
classical observables in the weak gravitational systems
by calculating scattering amplitude
BernC1
CalAmp-Rev
. In these toy models, the macroscopic star or black holes are treated
as super massive particles. Their classical gravitational potential
is be obtained by computing the four-point scattering amplitudes,
while the testable gravitational wave signals can be calculated in
