term of the five-point scattering amplitudes. This motivates us to study the scattering amplitudes of the
super massive KK states through the gravitational interactions
and examine their behavior in the nonrelativistic limit.

3.5.1. Nonrelativistic Scattering
Amplitudes of KK Gauge Bosons/Gravitons

We consider the four-point KK graviton amplitudes
in the nonrelativistic limit under low energy expansion

M_{n}^{2}\!\gg\!q^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
, where

\hskip 0.85358ptq\hskip 0.85358pt
denotes the magnitude of the 3-momentum of the
initial or final states of KK particles. For the inelastic channel
\{0,0,n,n\}
,
we have the magnitude of the 3-momentum of the
massless initial state
\hskip 0.85358ptq\!=\!E_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt

and the magnitude of the 3-momentum of the KK final state

\hskip 0.85358ptq^{\prime}\!=\!\hskip-0.85358pt\sqrt{E_{n}^{2}\!-\!M_{n}^{2}\,}\!\ll\!M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, with the full KK gauge boson scattering amplitude
in Eq.(
55
) and Eq.(
204
)
(Appendix
B
), we derive the
following expanded nonrelativistic results at the LO and NLO:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}[A_{\pm 1}^{a0}A_{\mp 1}^{b0}A_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358ptg^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip-0.85358pt-\mathcal{C}_{t})(1\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(100a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{T}[A_{\pm 1}^{a0}A_{\mp 1}^{b0}A_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\,g^{2}\hskip 0.85358ptq^{\prime}\,}{~2M_{n}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt(c_{\theta}-c_{3\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(100b)

where we have applied the 5d orbifold compactification
of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Then, using the full KK graviton scattering amplitude
(
219
) of Appendix
C
, we derive the
following expanded nonrelativistic results at the LO and NLO:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h^{0}_{\pm 2}h^{0}_{\mp 2}h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h^{0}_{\pm 2}h^{0}_{\mp 2}h^{\pm n}_{L}h^{\mp n}_{L}\big]=\frac{~3\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa^{2}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n}^{2}\,}{32}(3\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{4\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(101a)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\delta\mathcal{M}\big[h^{0}_{\pm 2}h^{0}_{\mp 2}h^{n}_{L}h^{n}_{L}\big]

\displaystyle=\delta\mathcal{M}\big[h^{0}_{\pm 2}h^{0}_{\mp 2}h^{\pm n}_{L}h^{\mp n}_{L}\big]=\frac{~3\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa^{2}q^{\prime\hskip 0.85358pt2}\,}{128}(2\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\!+c_{6\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(101b)

We see that for the inelastic channel of
\{0,0,n,n\}
, the LO
KK amplitude is of
O(M_{n}^{2}q^{0})
and the NLO KK amplitude has

O(M_{n}^{0}q^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Next, we consider the nonrelativistic limit of the
elastic KK gauge boson/graviton scattering channel

\{n,n,n,n\}
. With the full KK gauge boson scattering amplitude
in Eq.(
71
) and
Eqs. (
213
)-(
217
)
(Appendix
B
), we derive the
following expanded nonrelativistic results at the LO and NLO:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pt0}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}A_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]

\displaystyle=\frac{~g^{2}M_{n}^{2}~}{q^{2}}\!\left[\mathcal{C}_{t}\frac{~1\hskip-0.85358pt+c_{2\theta}~}{1\hskip-0.85358pt+c_{\theta}}+\mathcal{C}_{u}\frac{\,-(1+c_{2\theta})~}{1\hskip-0.85358pt-c_{\theta}}\right]\!,

(102a)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\delta\mathcal{T}[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}A_{L}^{cn}A_{L}^{dn}]

\displaystyle=g^{2}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left[\mathcal{C}_{s}\frac{\hskip 0.85358pt-9\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\,}{2}+\mathcal{C}_{t}\frac{\,4\!-\!19\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt6\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{3\theta}}{4\hskip 0.85358pt(1\hskip-0.85358pt+c_{\theta})}+\mathcal{C}_{u}\frac{\,-(4\!+\!19\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt6\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{3\theta})\,}{4\hskip 0.85358pt(1\hskip-0.85358pt-c_{\theta})}\hskip-0.85358pt\right]\hskip-0.85358pt\!.\hskip-5.69054pt

(102b)

We note that the LO elastic KK gauge boson amplitude
\mathcal{T}_{0}
has

O(M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358ptq^{-2})
and exhibits a low energy behavior of

\,1\hskip-0.85358pt/q^{2}\,
due to the exchange of massless zero modes
in the
t
and
u
channels with the momentum transfer

Q^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt2q^{2}(1\!\pm\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})
,
where
Q^{2}\!=\!-t
or
Q^{2}\!=\!-u\hskip 0.85358pt
. It is worth to note that after Fourier transformation,
this
\,1\hskip-0.85358pt/Q^{2}\,
behavior reproduces
the classical Coulomb potential
1/\hskip 0.85358ptr\,
.

Then, using the double-copied full KK graviton amplitudes
(
230
)-(
234
)
of Appendix
C
, we derive the
following expanded nonrelativistic partial amplitudes at the LO:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\mp n}\big]

\displaystyle=0\,,

(103a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\pm n}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}\hskip 0.85358pt(11\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt12\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+9\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta})~}{16\hskip 0.85358pt(1\hskip-0.85358pt-c_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358ptq^{2}}\,,

(103b)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}\hskip 0.85358pt(11\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt12c_{\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+9c_{2\theta})~}{16\hskip 0.85358pt(1\hskip-0.85358pt+c_{\theta})\hskip 0.85358ptq^{2}}\,.

(103c)

Under the 5d orbifold compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
,
we further deduce the following LO amplitude with
the external KK states being
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip-0.85358pt
even:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{n}h_{L}^{n}h_{L}^{n}h_{L}^{n}\big]\!

\displaystyle=\hskip-0.85358pt\mbox{$\frac{\,{1}\,}{\,2\,}$}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\left(\!\mathcal{M}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\mp n}\big]\!+\!{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\pm n}\big]\!+\!{\mathcal{M}}_{0}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}\big]\!\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}~}{8\hskip 1.42262ptq^{2}}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358pt3\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta})^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta\hskip 0.85358pt.

(104)

We see that these LO elastic KK amplitudes have

O(M_{n}^{4}\hskip 0.85358ptq^{-2})
and they exhibit a low energy behavior of

\,1/q^{2}\,
. This is due to the exchange of massless zero modes
in the
t
and/or
u
channels with the momentum transfer

Q^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt2q^{2}(1\!\pm\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})
,
where
Q^{2}\!=\!-t
or
Q^{2}\!=\!-u\hskip 0.85358pt
. It is worth to note that after Fourier transformation,
this
\,1\hskip-0.85358pt/Q^{2}\,
behavior reproduces
the classical Newtonian gravitational potential
1/\hskip 0.85358ptr\,
.

Then, under the nonrelativistic expansion,
we derive the following NLO KK amplitude for
the elastic scattering channel
\{n,n,n,n\}
:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\delta\mathcal{M}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\mp n}\big]

\displaystyle=0\,,

(105a)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\delta\mathcal{M}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\pm n}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}}{\,64\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\,}(102\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt22\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-0.85358pt72\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-0.85358pt51\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3\theta}\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-0.85358pt18\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-0.85358pt9\hskip 0.85358ptc_{5\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(105b)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\delta\mathcal{M}\big[h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}h_{L}^{\pm n}h_{L}^{\mp n}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}}{\,64\hskip 0.85358pt(1\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})\,}(102\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt22\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-0.85358pt72\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt51\hskip 0.85358ptc_{3\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt18\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt9\hskip 0.85358ptc_{5\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(105c)

which are of
O(M_{n}^{2}q^{0})
and energy-independent. For the 5d orbifold compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
,
we further derive the corresponding NLO elastic KK amplitude:

\delta\mathcal{M}\big[h_{L}^{n}h_{L}^{n}h_{L}^{n}h_{L}^{n}\big]=\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}~}{128}\hskip 0.85358pt(226\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt217\hskip 0.85358ptc_{\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt78\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt9\hskip 0.85358ptc_{6\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta\,,

(106)

which also has the magnitude of
O(M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358ptq^{0})
.

3.5.2. Nonrelativistic KK Scattering Amplitudes
with KK Graviton Exchange

For comparison with the above nonrelativistic pure
KK gauge/gravity amplitudes, we consider the
Einstein gravity coupled to scalar fields or gauge fields
under the 5d KK compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

and analyze the nonrelativistic scattering amplitudes of
KK scalars (KK gauge bosons) through the KK graviton exchanges.

We first consider the 5d Einstein-Scalar (ES) theory as a toy model,
which contains a massless real scalar field
\widehat{\Phi}

coupled to gravity:

S_{\mathrm{ES}}\,=\,\int\!\!\mathrm{d}^{4}\hskip-0.85358pt{x}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{d}y\,\sqrt{\!-\hat{g}\,}\!\left(\!\frac{2}{~\hat{\kappa}^{2}\,}\widehat{R}+\frac{1}{\,2\,}\hat{g}_{\mu\nu}\partial^{\mu}\widehat{\Phi}\hskip 0.85358pt\partial^{\nu}\widehat{\Phi}\hskip-0.85358pt\right)\!,

(107)

Under the 5d compactification, we make the KK expansion
of
\widehat{\Phi}
and derive the trilinear
KK scalar-graviton interaction vertex

\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n}
\hskip-0.85358pt-
\phi_{m}
\hskip-0.85358pt-
h_{k}^{\mu\nu}

and the KK scalar-radion interaction vertex

\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n}
\hskip-0.85358pt-
\phi_{m}
\hskip-0.85358pt-
h_{0}^{55}
. Thus, we can compute the four-point scalar scattering amplitudes
with zero-mode and KK graviton-exchanges as well as the
radion exchange. For the inelastic channel

\phi_{0}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{0}\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{n}
,
we derive the following scattering amplitude:

\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}[\phi_{0}\phi_{0}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]\,=\,-\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\big[(7\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip-0.85358pt+(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]^{2}~}{32\hskip 0.85358pt\big[(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-1.42262pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\big]}\,,

(108)

where

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pts/M_{n}^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{q}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\prime\hskip 0.85358pt2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt1)\hskip 0.85358pt

and
\bar{q}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt{q}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt/M_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
. We have verified the above amplitude by using the shifting method
of Sec.
2.2
to derive the massive KK amplitude from
computing the corresponding 5d massless amplitude. Then, from Eq.(
108
)
we make the nonrelativistic expansion

\hskip 0.85358ptq^{\prime\hskip 0.85358pt2}\!\ll\!M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and derive the following LO and NLO scalar amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}^{(0)}[\phi_{0}\phi_{0}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt-\frac{\,9\,}{\,4\,}{\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}}\,,

(109a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}[\phi_{0}\phi_{0}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt-\frac{\,3\,}{\,8\,}{\kappa^{2}q^{\hskip 0.85358pt\prime\hskip 0.85358pt2}}(11\!+5\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(109b)

We see that the LO and NLO amplitudes are of

O(\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2})
and

O(\kappa^{2}q^{\hskip 0.85358pt\prime\hskip 0.85358pt2})
, respectively.

For the elastic channel
\{n,n,n,n\}
,
we compute the KK scalar scattering amplitude as follows:

\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(X_{0}\!+\!X_{2}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\!+\!X_{4}c_{4\theta}\!+\!X_{6}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{6\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{~512\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)\big[(\bar{s}^{2}+24\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}+16)\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)^{2}c_{2\theta}\big]~}\,,

(110)

where

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pts/M_{n}^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{q}^{2}\!+\!1)\hskip 0.85358pt

with
\bar{q}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt{q}/M_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and the polynomial functions
\{X_{j}\}
take the following form:

\displaystyle X_{0}

\displaystyle=510\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt8144\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt27584\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!+61440\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!+60928\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}+\hskip-0.85358pt28672\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=-429\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+6984\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt30816\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt51712\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt42240\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt34816\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle X_{4}

\displaystyle=-78\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt1200\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt6720\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt16384\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!15872\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4096\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle X_{6}

\displaystyle=-3\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt56\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt416\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt1536\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2816\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt2048\,.

Thus, making the nonrelativistic expansion

\hskip 0.85358ptq^{2}\!\ll\!M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
we derive the following KK scalar scattering amplitudes
at the LO and NLO:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}^{(0)}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358pt-\frac{~2\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{q^{2}}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(112a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}[\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358pt-\frac{1}{\,2\,}\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt{(7\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})}\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta\hskip 0.85358pt,

(112b)

whose magnitudes are of
O(\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}/q^{2})
and

O(\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2})
, respectively.
Note that the LO elastic KK scalar amplitude
(
112a
) also exhibits a low energy behavior of

\,1/q^{2}\,
due to the exchange of massless zero modes
in the
t
and
u
channels with the momentum transfer

Q^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt2q^{2}(1\!\pm\hskip-0.85358ptc_{\theta})
,
where
Q^{2}\!=\!-t
or
Q^{2}\!=\!-u\hskip 0.85358pt
. This is similar to the LO amplitudes of the pure KK gauge boson
(KK graviton) scattering in
Eqs.(
102a
) and (
3.5
). After a Fourier transformation
such
\,1\hskip-0.85358pt/Q^{2}\,
behavior recovers the classical
Newtonian gravitational potential of
1\hskip-0.85358pt/r
at low energy. We also see that the NLO KK amplitude
(
112b
) behaves as
q
-independent constant term.

Next, for comparison we further consider the 5d gauge theory
coupled to GR, which has the following
5d action:

S_{\mathrm{GRA}}\,=\,\int\!\!\mathrm{d}^{4}\hskip-0.85358pt{x}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{d}y\,\sqrt{\!-\hat{g}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\left(\!\frac{2}{~\hat{\kappa}^{2}\,}\widehat{R}-\frac{1}{\,4\,}\hat{g}^{\mu\alpha}\hat{g}^{\nu\beta}\widehat{F}^{\hskip 0.85358pta}_{\mu\nu}\widehat{F}^{\hskip 0.85358pta}_{\alpha\beta}\right)\!,

(113)

where
\widehat{F}^{\hskip 0.85358pta}_{\mu\nu}
is the 5d gauge field strength of a
non-Abelian gauge group of YM theory or
Abelian gauge group of Maxwell theory (with
a\!=\!1
). Under the 5d compactification, we derive the 4d KK theory
which includes the trilinear KK gauge-boson-graviton interaction
vertex
\hskip 0.85358ptA_{n}^{a\hskip 0.85358pt\mu}
\hskip-0.85358pt-
A_{m}^{b\hskip 0.85358pt\nu}
\hskip-0.85358pt-
h_{k}^{\mu\nu}
. Then, we will compute the four-point KK gauge boson scattering
amplitudes with graviton exchange and derive the nonrelativistic
results at low energy, which should be compared to the
nonrelativistic KK scalar amplitudes we derived for the
Einstein-Scalar theory (
107
). For the case of non-Abelian gauge theory, the four-point KK gauge
boson amplitudes will also receive the contribution
by pure gauge interactions including the contact gauge vertex and the
gauge boson exchange via cubic gauge vertex. But such pure gauge
contribution is gauge-invariant by itself and has already been
computed in Sec. 3.5.1 and Sec.
3.2
-
3.3
. So, in the following we will focus on analyzing the
nonrelativistic KK gauge boson amplitudes through the graviton
exchanges and compare them with the nonrelativistic KK scalar amplitudes
which we derived in the first part of Sec. 3.5.2.

For the inelastic channel
\{0,0,n,n\}
, we compute the
four-point gauge boson scattering amplitude via graviton
and radion exchanges:

\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}\big[A_{\pm 1}^{a0}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{a0}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{bn}\!A_{L}^{bn}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\big[(3\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!+\!18\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt8)\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(3\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!14\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt8)\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]~}{8\hskip 0.85358pt\big[(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip 0.85358pt\big]}\,.

(114)

Then, we make the nonrelativistic expansion and derive
the LO and NLO scattering amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}^{(0)}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[A_{\pm 1}^{a0}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{a0}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{bn}\!A_{L}^{bn}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{7}{\,4\,}\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\,,

(115a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[A_{\pm 1}^{a0}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{a0}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{L}^{bn}\!A_{L}^{bn}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,4\,}\kappa^{2}q^{\hskip 0.85358pt\prime\hskip 0.85358pt2}(7+c_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(115b)

The above LO and NLO amplitudes are of
O(\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2})
and

O(\kappa^{2}q^{\hskip 0.85358pt\prime\hskip 0.85358pt2})
respectively. These have the same structures as that of the corresponding
LO and NLO scalar amplitudes in Eq.(
109
),
except that the angular dependence of
the above NLO amplitude (
115b
)
has different coefficients from that of
Eq.(
109b
).

For the elastic channel
\{n,n,n,n\}
, we first consider all
the external KK gauge bosons having transverse polarizations
with the helicity indices
(\pm 1,\pm 1,\pm 1,\pm 1)
. Thus, we compute their elastic scattering amplitude as follows:

\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}\big[A_{\pm 1}^{an}A_{\pm 1}^{an}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{bn}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{bn}\big]=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}\big[(3\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!11\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\!12)\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(\bar{s}\!-\!4)c_{2\theta}\big]~}{2\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}-4)}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(116)

where

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pts/M_{n}^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{q}^{2}\!+\!1)\hskip 0.85358pt

with
\bar{q}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt{q}/M_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Then, we make the nonrelativistic expansion and derive
the following LO and NLO elastic scattering amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}^{(0)}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[A_{\pm 1}^{an}A_{\pm 1}^{an}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{bn}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{bn}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}~}{2\hskip 0.85358ptq^{2}}\,,

(117a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}\big[A_{\pm 1}^{an}A_{\pm 1}^{an}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{bn}\hskip-0.85358ptA_{\pm 1}^{bn}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\,8\,}\kappa^{2}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt(9\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(117b)

We see that the LO nonrelativistic KK amplitude
(
117a
)
exhibits a low energy behavior

\,1/q^{2}\,
due to the exchange of massless zero modes
in the
t
and
u
channels
which is similar to the LO KK scalar amplitude
(
112a
). But, the above LO and NLO KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes have
different angular structures from that of the
elastic KK scalar scattering amplitudes (
112
).

Finally, we compute the four-point elastic longitudinal
KK gauge boson scattering amplitude via the graviton
and radion exchanges:

\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}\big[A_{L}^{an}\!A_{L}^{an}\!A_{L}^{bn}\!A_{L}^{bn}\big]=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\big(X_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptX_{2}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\!X_{4}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{4\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\!X_{6}\hskip 0.85358ptc_{6\theta}\big)\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta~}{~512\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)\big[(\bar{s}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt24\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\!16)\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4)^{2}c_{2\theta}\big]~}\,,

(118)

where

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\!=\!s/M_{n}^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt(\bar{q}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt1)\hskip 0.85358pt

with
\bar{q}\!=\!{q}/M_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and the polynomial functions
\{X_{j}\}
are expressed as follows:

\displaystyle X_{0}

\displaystyle=510\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt7696\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt35520\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt39936\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt40448\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt28672\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=-429\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt7528\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt25056\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!+77824\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\!11520\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt34816\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle X_{4}

\displaystyle=-78\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+\!1136\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt4928\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!+\!13312\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{2}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt28160\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt4096\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle X_{6}

\displaystyle=-3\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{5}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt24\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{4}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt32\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}^{3}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt768\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{s}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt2048\hskip 0.85358pt.

(119)

From the above, we make the nonrelativistic expansion and derive
the LO and NLO longitudinal KK scattering amplitudes as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}^{(0)}\big[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}A_{L}^{bn}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{~2\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}\cot^{2}\!\theta~}{q^{2}}=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}~}{q^{2}}(1\!+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{2\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta\hskip 0.85358pt,

(120a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{T}_{\hskip 0.85358pth}\big[A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{an}A_{L}^{bn}A_{L}^{bn}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{~\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2}~}{8}(23\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt8\hskip 0.85358ptc_{2\theta}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptc_{4\theta})\hskip-0.85358pt\csc^{2}\!\theta\hskip 0.85358pt.

(120b)

The above can be compared to the LO and NLO amplitudes
(
117
) with all the external KK states being
transversely polarized, and they have different angular structures. Moreover, comparing the above nonrelativistic LO and NLO
longitudinal KK scattering amplitudes with
the scalar KK scattering amplitudes (
112
),
we find that their LO amplitudes have the magnitude of

O(\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{4}/q^{2})
and their NLO amplitudes are of

O(\kappa^{2}M_{n}^{2})
, but they have rather different
angular structures at the LO and NLO respectively. These nontrivial differences of angular structures between
the nonrelativistic KK scalar scattering amplitudes
(
112
) and the KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes
(
117
) are due to the different spins of the
external KK states in the two cases and their different
cubic interaction vertices with the internal gravitons. Such differences remain even in the nonrelativistic KK scattering
amplitudes.

4
Worldsheet Formulation of Massive KK Double-Copy

The conventional Cachazo-He-Yuan (CHY) formalism
CHY

can be used to analyze the scattering amplitudes of
the massless scalar, gauge, and gravity theories
in terms of the localized integrals
in the moduli space
\mathfrak{M}_{0,N}
of
the
N
-punctured genus-zero Riemann sphere
\mathbb{CP}^{1}
. Its physical origin is based on the ambitwistor string theory
Mason:2013sva
;
Geyer:2014fka
;
Adamo:2013tsa
;
Casali:2015vta
.
In this section, we generalize the conventional massless
CHY scattering equations to the case of the massive KK scattering
amplitudes under the 5d toroidal compactification. We derive an extended massive KK scattering equation
for the KK scattering amplitudes in Section
4.1

and construct the KK bi-adjoint scalar amplitudes
in Section
4.2
. Then, in Section
4.3
we use this massive CHY approach to
further construct the scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons
and of KK gravitons, and derive their relations to the extended
BCJ-type mmasive KK amplitudes (given in Section
3
) and
to the extended KLT-type masive KK amplitudes
(formulated in Ref.
KKString
).

4.1
Extended CHY Formulation of
Massive KK Scattering Amplitudes

Consider the
(4\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\delta)
-dimensional spacetime with

\delta
extra spatial dimensions under the
toroidal compactification. For a momentum
\hat{p}_{a}
living in the

(4\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\delta)
-dimensions,
we can decompose it in the following form:

\hat{p}_{a}^{M}=\left(\hskip-0.85358ptp_{a}^{\mu},\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}}{\,R_{1}}\bigg|_{a}\hskip 0.85358pt,\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\frac{{\mathfrak{n}_{\delta}}}{\,R_{\delta}}\bigg|_{a}\right)\!,\hskip 10.00002pt~~a\in\{1,\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,N\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(121)

where
\,p^{\mu}_{a}\,
and

\,(\mathfrak{n}_{i}/R_{i})\big|_{a}\,
are the momenta defined in

(3\!+\!1)
-dimensions and
\delta
-dimensions respectively. For the simplicity of demonstration,
we set
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!\!=\hskip-1.42262pt\!1\hskip 0.85358pt

with the compactification radius

\hskip 0.85358ptR_{1}\!=\!R
. Hence, similar to
Dolan
Naculich

we can express the
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
invariant
scattering equation in the
(4\!+\!1)
-dimensional
spacetime as follows:

\widehat{E}_{a}\,=\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
(b\neq a)\end{subarray}}^{N}\!\!\frac{~\hat{p}_{a}\!\cdot\hat{p}_{b}~}{z_{a}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptz_{b}}\,=\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=1\\
(b\neq a)\end{subarray}}^{N}\!\!\frac{~p_{a}\!\cdot p_{b}+\!M^{2}_{ab}~}{z_{a}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptz_{b}}\,=\,0\,,

(122)

where the
N
holomorphic variables
\{z_{a}\}

parametrize the moduli space
\mathfrak{M}_{0,N}
of
the
N
-punctured Riemann sphere
\mathbb{CP}^{1}
,
and summation runs over the
\,(N\!-3)!\,

solutions for
z_{a}
. The mass parameter in Eq.(
122
) is given by

M_{ab}^{2}=M_{ba}^{2}=\frac{~\mathfrak{n}_{a}\mathfrak{n}_{b}~}{R^{2}}\,.

(123)

As analyzed in Section
2.1
, we find that
each massive KK amplitude can be decomposed into
a sum of higher dimensional sub-amplitudes in which
the components of momenta associated with the
compactified dimension are discretized. Hence, under the toroidal compactification,
we can generalize the conventional CHY formulation of
the gauge boson amplitude and graviton amplitude
to the following forms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{N}\big[\{p_{i},\hat{\zeta}_{i},I_{i}\}\big]

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{q_{j}}\!\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{N}\big[\{p_{i},\hat{\zeta}_{i},q_{i}\}\big]\hskip-0.85358pt\prod_{j}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\int_{\mathbb{N}}\text{d}y_{j}\hskip 0.85358pte^{\text{i}q_{j}y_{j}}\mathcal{O}_{I_{j}}\hskip-0.85358pt(y_{j})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(124a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{N}\big[\{p_{i},\hat{\zeta}_{i},I_{i}\}\big]

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{q_{j}}\!\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{N}\big[\{p_{i},\hat{\zeta}_{i},q_{i}\}\big]\hskip-0.85358pt\prod_{j}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\int_{\mathbb{N}}\text{d}y_{j}\hskip 0.85358pte^{\text{i}q_{j}y_{j}}\mathcal{O}_{I_{j}}\hskip-0.85358pt(y_{j})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(124b)

On the right-hand side of Eq.(
124
),
the amplitude
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt
is defined as

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{N}\big[\{\hat{p},\hat{\zeta}\}\big]=\,\int\!\!\hskip-0.85358pt\underbrace{\frac{\text{d}^{N}\!z}{\,\mathrm{Vol}\hskip 0.85358pt[\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})]\,}\sideset{}{{}^{\prime}}{\prod}_{a=1}^{N}\!\delta\big(\widehat{E}_{a}\big)}_{\text{d}\mu_{N}}\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{N}(\{\hat{p},\hat{\zeta},z\})\,,

(125)

where
\text{d}\mu_{N}
is the measure and
\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{N}
is a theory-dependent integrand. The above product operation
\prod^{\prime}

is defined by

\sideset{}{{}^{\prime}}{\prod}_{a=1}^{N}\!\delta\big(\widehat{E}_{a}\big)\,\equiv\,(z_{i}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptz_{j})(z_{j}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptz_{k})(z_{k}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptz_{i})\hskip-8.82036pt\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a=1\\
(a\neq i,j,k)\end{subarray}}^{N}\hskip-10.81204pt\delta\big(\widehat{E}_{a}\big)\hskip 0.85358pt,

(126)

which excludes the choice of the three fixed points

(z_{i},z_{j},z_{k})\!=\!(0,1,\infty)

on the Riemann sphere and is permutation invariant
CHY
. The scattering amplitude (
125
) is invariant
under
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
transformation acting on the coordinates

\{z_{a}\}
. Moreover, it is well established
that the conventional KLT relations
for the
(4\!+\!1)
-dimensional
scattering amplitudes of the massless gauge bosons
(
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{N}
) and of the massless gavitons
(
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{N}
) can be given by the CHY formalism:

\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt=\!\sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}}\!\!\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{N}[\alpha]\hskip 1.42262pt\widehat{\mathcal{K}}\hskip 1.42262pt[\alpha|\beta]\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{N}[\beta]\,,

(127)

where
\{\alpha,\beta\}\!\in\!S_{\hskip-0.85358ptN-3}\hskip 0.85358pt

denote the linearly independent
color-orderings of the
N
-point gauge boson amplitudes,
and
\widehat{\mathcal{K}}\hskip 1.42262pt[\alpha|\beta]
is the KLT kernel. Thus, combining the
(4\hskip-0.85358pt+\!1)
-dimensional
massless KLT formula (
127
) with
Eq.(
124
), we derive the
extended massive KLT-type relation under the
toroidal compactification, which expresses the KK graviton
scattering amplitude (
\mathcal{M}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}
) as products of two
color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitudes (
\mathcal{A}_{N}
)
together with a kernel (
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}\hskip 0.85358pt
) as follows:

\hskip-8.53581pt\mathcal{M}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}\big[{\{p_{k},\zeta^{\mu\nu}_{\sigma_{k}}\}}\big]\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358ptc_{0}\hskip-0.85358pt\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\!\sum_{\{\alpha,\hskip 0.85358pt\beta\}}\!\sum_{\lambda_{k}\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\!\Big(\!\prod_{k}\!C^{\,\sigma_{k}}_{\lambda_{k}\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\Big)\mathcal{A}_{N}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\alpha,\hskip 0.85358pt\zeta^{\mu}_{\lambda_{k}}\!,\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big]\mathcal{K}\!\left([\alpha|\beta],\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\}\hskip-0.85358pt\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{A}_{N}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\beta,\hskip 0.85358pt\zeta^{\nu}_{\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\!,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(128)

where
\hskip 0.85358ptc_{0}\!=\!(-)^{N+1}\hskip-0.85358pt[\kappa/(4g)]^{N-2}
is
the conversion constant between the gauge and gravity couplings
as given by Eq.(
23
). In the above,

\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}
denote the KK indices of the external states
and
\mathsf{P}
labels every possible combination of the signs of
these KK indices
\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}
which obeying the

N
-point neutral condition

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathfrak{n}_{i}\!=\!0

KKString
. In Eq.(
128
),
k
counts the external states
of each amplitude and

C_{\lambda_{k}\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}^{\sigma_{k}}
denotes the coefficients
of the polarization tensor
\hskip 0.85358pt\zeta^{\mu\nu}_{\sigma_{\hskip-0.85358ptk}}

of the
k
-th external KK graviton as defined
in Eqs.(
21
) and (
39
). Finally, we note that
the above formulation can be readily extended to the
case of having two or more compactified extra dimensions.

In summary, our above analysis has extended
the conventional massless CHY formulation
of the KLT double-copy relations
to the compactified massive KK gauge/gravity theories. In this massive KK CHY construction, the KLT kernel

\mathcal{K}\!\left([\alpha|\beta],\!\{\mathfrak{n}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\right)

is formulated as the inverse of amplitudes of the KK
bi-ajoint scalar theory under the toroidal compactification
and will be discussed in Section
4.3
.

4.2
CHY Construction for Massive KK Bi-ajoint Scalar Amplitudes

Consider a massless bi-adjoint scalar (BAS) theory
respecting the (global) color symmetry

\mathrm{U}(N)\!\otimes\!\mathrm{U}({N})\hskip 0.85358pt
. The simplest Lagrangian of such a BAS theory
contains a cubic interaction term
CHY
. We can write down its Lagrangian formulation in 5d as follows:

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{BAS}}\,=\,\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{M}\widehat{\Phi}^{aa^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt)^{2}+\frac{\hat{\lambda}}{\,3!\,}\hskip-0.85358ptf^{abc}f^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358ptc^{\prime}}\widehat{\Phi}^{aa^{\prime}}\widehat{\Phi}^{bb^{\prime}}\widehat{\Phi}^{cc^{\prime}}\!,

(129)

where
\hskip 0.85358pt\hat{\lambda}\hskip 0.85358pt
is the 5d coupling for the cubic scalar interaction.

Then, under the 5d KK compactification of
S^{1}
,
we make the following KK expansion for the bi-adjoint scalar field

\widehat{\Phi}^{ab}
:

\widehat{\Phi}^{ab}(x^{\mu},y)\,=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi R\,}\,}\!\!\sum_{\mathfrak{n}=-\infty}^{+\infty}\!\!\Phi_{\mathfrak{n}}^{ab}(x^{\mu})\hskip 0.85358pte^{\text{i}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}\hskip 0.85358pty/\!R}\,.

(130)

Substituting the expansion (
130
)
into the BAS Lagrangian (
129
)
and integrating over the 5d coordinate
y\hskip 0.85358pt
, we can
derive the effective KK Lagrangian:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{BAS}}^{\mathrm{KK}}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\,2\,}|\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{0}^{aa^{\prime}}|^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{\,6\,}f^{abc}\hskip-0.85358ptf^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\Phi^{aa^{\prime}}_{0}\Phi^{bb^{\prime}}_{0}\Phi^{cc^{\prime}}_{0}+\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\neq 0}\left(\frac{1}{\,2\,}|\partial_{\mu}\Phi_{\mathfrak{n}}^{aa^{\prime}}|^{2}+\frac{1}{\,2\,}M_{\mathfrak{n}}^{2}|\Phi_{\mathfrak{n}}^{aa^{\prime}}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{\,6\,}f^{abc}f^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c^{\prime}}\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\mathfrak{n}_{1},\mathfrak{n}_{2},\mathfrak{n}_{3}\neq 0}\!\!\left(\Phi^{aa^{\prime}}_{0}\Phi^{bb^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\Phi^{cc^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\delta_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}+\mathfrak{n}_{2},0}+\Phi^{aa^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}\Phi^{bb^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}\Phi^{cc^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}\delta_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}+\mathfrak{n}_{2}+\mathfrak{n}_{3},0}\right)\!,

(131)

where the effctive 4d KK scalar coupling

\,\lambda\!=\!\hat{\lambda}/\hskip-1.42262pt\sqrt{2\pi R\,}
.

From the CHY fomulation (
125
), the full scattering amplitude
in the massless 5d bi-adjoint scalar theory
takes the following form:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{N}^{\mathrm{BAS}}=\int\!\!\text{d}\mu_{N}\!\!\sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}}\!\!\text{PT}[\alpha]\,\mathcal{C}[\alpha]\!\times\!\text{PT}[\beta]\,\mathcal{C}[\beta]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(132)

In the above, the integration measure
\text{d}\mu_{N}\hskip-0.85358pt
is defined
in Eq.(
125
) and can be computed according to the massive
scattering equations (
122
)-(
123
)
which include the KK masses via discretized fifth momentum-components. In Eq.(
132
),
the factor
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}[\alpha]
is decomposed into
the Del Duca-Dixon-Maltoni (DDM) basis
DDM

which contains
(N\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2)!\hskip 0.85358pt
elements:

\mathcal{C}[\alpha]\,=\sum_{c_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358ptc_{N-3}}\hskip-11.38109ptf^{a_{1}a_{\alpha(2)}c_{1}}f^{c_{1}a_{\alpha(3)}c_{2}}\cdots f^{c_{N-3}a_{\alpha(N-1)}a_{N}}\,,

(133)

where the ordering of the 1st and
N
-th labels are fixed
at the beginning and end in the ordering
\alpha\hskip 0.85358pt
, i.e.,

\alpha\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\![1,\alpha(2),\cdots\!,\alpha(N\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!1),N]\!\hskip-0.85358pt\in\hskip-0.85358pt\!S_{N-2}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and the ordering
\beta
is defined in the similar way. We also use the same label
\alpha
to denote the possible
orderings and the permutations. In Eq.(
132
), the Parke-Taylor factor

\text{PT}[\alpha]\hskip 0.85358pt
is defined as

\text{PT}[\alpha]\,=\,\frac{1}{~z_{1\alpha(2)}\cdots\hskip 0.85358ptz_{\alpha(N-1)N}\hskip 0.85358ptz_{N1}~}\,,

(134)

where in the denominator each factor

\,z_{ij}\!\!=\!z_{i}\!-\hskip-1.42262ptz_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt
. As for a consistent check, consider the case of four-point scattering
amplitudes, which have two allowed color orderings:

\alpha\!=\![1234]
and
\beta\!=\![1324]\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, the color factors defined in Eq.(
133
)
are connected to the definitions (
29
) via

\mathcal{C}[1234]=\mathcal{C}_{s}\,,\hskip 20.00003pt\mathcal{C}[1324]=-\mathcal{C}_{u}\,,

(135)

where the third color factor

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{t}\!=\!-\hskip 1.42262pt\mathcal{C}_{s}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{u}
is determined by
using the color Jacobi identity in Eq.(
30
). For demonstration, we first consider the elastic four-point
KK BAS scattering amplitude. To solve the scattering equation (
122
) for
the present case, we fix

(z_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptz_{2},\hskip 0.85358ptz_{3})\!=\!(0,1,+\infty)

and derive the following solutions of
\hskip 0.85358ptz_{4}\hskip 0.85358pt

for the three independent combinations of
KK indices of the external states respectively:

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{1}=\{\pm n,\pm n,\mp n,\mp n\}\!:

\displaystyle~~~~~z_{4}=-\frac{u}{~s\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}~}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(136a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{2}=\{\pm n,\mp n,\mp n,\pm n\}\!:

\displaystyle~~~~~z_{4}=-\frac{~u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}~}{s}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(136b)

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{3}=\{\pm n,\mp n,\pm n,\mp n\}\!:

\displaystyle~~~~~z_{4}=-\frac{\,u\,}{s}\,.

(136c)

With these, we compute the double partial amplitudes
of the KK bi-adjoint scalars for each KK combination
\mathsf{P}_{i}

as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[1^{\pm n}2^{\pm n}3^{\mp n}4^{\mp n}|1^{\pm n}2^{\pm n}3^{\mp n}4^{\mp n}]

\displaystyle=-2\hskip 0.85358pt\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{\,s\!-\!4M_{n}^{2}\,}+\!\frac{1}{\,t\,}\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{1}}\hskip-0.85358pt|\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{1}}]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(137a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[1^{\pm n}2^{\mp n}3^{\mp n}4^{\pm n}|1^{\pm n}2^{\mp n}3^{\mp n}4^{\pm n}]

\displaystyle=-2\hskip 0.85358pt\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{\,s\,}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,t\!-\!4M_{n}^{2}\,}\hskip-1.42262pt\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{2}}\hskip-0.85358pt|\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{2}}]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(137b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[1^{\pm n}2^{\mp n}3^{\pm n}4^{\mp n}|1^{\pm n}2^{\mp n}3^{\pm n}4^{\mp n}]

\displaystyle=-2\hskip 0.85358pt\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{\,s\,}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,t\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{3}}|\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{3}}]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(137c)

Then, summing up the contributions of the three combinations above,
we further derive the double partial KK-amplitude
under the
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
compactification:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[1^{n}2^{n}3^{n}4^{n}|1^{n}2^{n}3^{n}4^{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,2\,}\sum_{i}{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{i}}|\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{i}}]\hskip 0.85358pt\equiv\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[\alpha^{n}|\alpha^{n}]

\displaystyle=-\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{\,s\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}\,}\!+\!\frac{1}{\,t\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}\,}\!+\!\frac{2}{\,s\,}+\frac{2}{\,t\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\right)\!.

(138)

Similarly, for the other two double partial KK-amplitudes,
we derive the following:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[\alpha^{n}|\beta^{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,2\,}\sum_{i}{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha_{\mathsf{P}_{i}}|\beta_{\mathsf{P}_{i}}]=\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{~t\!-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}~}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{2}{\,t\,}\right)\!,

(139a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[\beta^{n}|\beta^{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\,2\,}\sum_{i}{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\beta_{\mathsf{P}_{i}}|\beta_{\mathsf{P}_{i}}]=-\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{\,t\!-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}}\!+\!\frac{1}{\,u\!-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}\,}\!+\!\frac{2}{\,t\,}\!+\!\frac{2}{\,u\,}\hskip-0.85358pt\right)\!,

(139b)

where we have denoted

[\alpha^{n}]\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt[1^{n}2^{n}3^{n}4^{n}]
and

[\beta^{n}]\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt[1^{n}3^{n}2^{n}4^{n}]\hskip 0.85358pt
.

From the above,
we can compute the four-point elastic KK BAS scattering amplitude
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}\big[\Phi_{n}^{aa^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Phi_{n}^{bb^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Phi_{n}^{cc^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Phi_{n}^{dd^{\prime}}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[\alpha^{n}|\alpha^{n}]-(\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}+\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime})\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[\alpha^{n}|\beta^{n}]+\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[\beta^{n}|\beta^{n}]

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt\lambda^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}\!\left(\!\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{~s_{j}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt4M_{n}^{2}~}+\frac{2\,}{\,s_{j}\,}\!\right)\!,

(140)

where the color factors
\{\mathcal{C}_{j}\}
(
\hskip 0.85358ptj\!=\!s,t,u\hskip 0.85358pt
)
are defined in Eq.(
29
), and

\{\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\prime}\}
are obtained from
\{\mathcal{C}_{j}\}
by replacing the
hidden color indices
(a,b,c,d)\!\rightarrow\!(a^{\prime},b^{\prime},c^{\prime},d^{\prime})\hskip 0.85358pt
. In the second line of Eq.(
140
) we have used
a color identity

\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}+\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt=\mathcal{C}_{t}\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\prime}-\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime}-\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}
. Eq.(
140
) shows that after simplification
the scattering amplitude of the KK bi-adjoint scalars
takes a BCJ-like form.
Inspecting the explicit formulas of the KK gauge boson amplitude
(
71
) and of the KK graviton amplitude
(
72
) for the elastic scattering
\{n,n,n,n\}
,
we see that
apart from the group factors
\,\mathcal{C}_{j}
(
j\!=\!s,t,u\hskip 0.85358pt
)
and numerators
\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathsf{P}}
(
\mathsf{P}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},\mathsf{C}
),
the summed KK amplitudes of Eq.(
71
) or
Eq.(
72
) contain the massive kernels

\,1/(s_{j}\!-\!M_{n}^{2})\,
and massless kenels
\,1/s_{j}\,

in each channel of
(s,\hskip 0.85358ptt,\hskip 0.85358ptu)
, which match
the corresponding kernel of the above elastic KK BAS amplitude
(
140
), respectively.

Next, we can further compute the inelastic KK BAS scattering
amplitude of
(0,0)\!\rightarrow\!(n,n)\hskip 0.85358pt
. For this process, we derive the solution of the massive
scattering equation as follows:

\mathsf{P}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\{0,0,\pm n,\mp n\}\!:~~~~z_{4}=-\frac{~u\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{n}^{2}~}{s}\,.

(141)

Then, we derive the following color-ordered
inelastic double amplitudes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[1^{0}2^{0}3^{n}4^{n}|1^{0}2^{0}3^{n}4^{n}]

\displaystyle=-2\hskip 0.85358pt\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{\,s\,}+\frac{1}{\,t\!-\!M_{n}^{2}\,}\!\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha^{\hskip-0.85358pt0n}|\alpha^{\hskip-0.85358pt0n}]\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[1^{0}2^{0}3^{n}4^{n}|1^{0}3^{0}2^{n}4^{n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{2\hskip 0.85358pt\lambda^{2}}{~t\hskip-0.85358pt-\!M_{n}^{2}~}\equiv\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha^{\hskip-0.85358pt0n}|\beta^{0n}]\hskip 0.85358pt,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[1^{0}3^{0}2^{n}4^{n}|1^{0}3^{0}2^{n}4^{n}]

\displaystyle=-2\hskip 0.85358pt\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{~t\!-\!M_{n}^{2}~}+\frac{1}{~u\!-\!M_{n}^{2}~}\!\right)\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\beta^{0n}|\beta^{0n}]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(142)

With these, we can compute the massive KK BAS scattering amplitude
of the inelastic channel

\Phi_{0}^{aa^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Phi_{0}^{bb^{\prime}}\!\!\rightarrow\!\Phi_{n}^{cc^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Phi_{n}^{dd^{\prime}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\Phi_{0}^{aa^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Phi_{0}^{bb^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Phi_{n}^{cc^{\prime}}\hskip-0.85358pt\Phi_{n}^{dd^{\prime}}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime}{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha^{\hskip-0.85358pt0n}|\alpha^{\hskip-0.85358pt0n}]\!-\!(\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}\!+\!\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime}){\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\alpha^{0n}|\beta^{0n}]+\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}{\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}}[\beta^{0n}|\beta^{0n}]

\displaystyle=-2\hskip 0.85358pt\lambda^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\,\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime}\,}{s}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{t}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\prime}}{\,t\!-\!M_{n}^{2}\,}+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}}{\,u\!-\!M_{n}^{2}~}\!\right)\!,

(143)

where in the last step we have also applied the
color identity

\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}+\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt=\mathcal{C}_{t}\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\prime}-\mathcal{C}_{s}\mathcal{C}_{s}^{\prime}-\mathcal{C}_{u}\mathcal{C}_{u}^{\prime}
. Inspecting our explicit formulas of the KK gauge boson amplitude
(
55
) and of the KK graviton amplitude (
3.2
)
for the inelastic scattering
\{0,0,n,n\}
, we see that
apart from the group factors
\mathcal{C}_{j}
and numerators

\mathcal{N}_{j}^{\mathsf{A}}
, these KK amplitudes contain the kernels

(1/s,\,1/(t\!-\!M_{n}^{2}),\,1/(u\!-\!M_{n}^{2}))

in each channel of
(s,\hskip 0.85358ptt,\hskip 0.85358ptu)
, which equal
each kernel of the above KK BAS amplitude (
143
),
respectively.

For the
N
-point (
N\!\hskip-1.42262pt\geqslant\!5\hskip 0.85358pt
) KK bi-adjoint scalar amplitudes
of the BAS theory, it is much more complicated to directly calculate
the color-ordered scattering amplitude by Eq.(
125
). But according to the labeled tree method based on the Cayley functions
Gao:2017dek
,
the color-ordered BAS amplitude

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[\alpha|\beta]

of massless BAS theory is given by the common part of
cubic trees respecting the ordering
\alpha
and
\beta
up to
a factor
(-1)^{(N-3)+\mathrm{flip}}
. For the 5d massless BAS theory, the five-point
BAS amplitudes have two linearly independent double amplitudes
with color-ordering
[\alpha]\!=\![12345]
and
[\beta]\!=\![13254]
:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{S}}[12345|12345]

\displaystyle=\hat{\lambda}^{3}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{12}\hat{s}_{34}\,}+\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{34}\hat{s}_{15}\,}+\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{15}\hat{s}_{23}\,}+\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{23}\hat{s}_{45}\,}+\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{45}\hat{s}_{12}\,}\!\right)\!,

(144a)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{S}}[13254|13254]

\displaystyle=\hat{\lambda}^{3}\!\left(\!\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{13}\hat{s}_{25}\,}+\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{25}\hat{s}_{14}\,}+\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{14}\hat{s}_{23}\,}+\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{23}\hat{s}_{45}\,}+\frac{1}{\,\hat{s}_{45}\hat{s}_{13}\,}\!\right)\!,

(144b)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{S}}[12345|13254]

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{\lambda}^{3}}{\,\hat{s}_{23}\hat{s}_{45}\,}\,.

(144c)

From the above and using the shifting method established
in Section
2
, we can derive corresponding color-ordered
massive KK BAS amplitudes for the scattering process

(\mathfrak{n}_{1},\mathfrak{n}_{2})\!\rightarrow\!(\mathfrak{n}_{3},\mathfrak{n}_{4},\mathfrak{n}_{5})

with
\sum_{j=1}^{5}\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{j}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
under
5d compactification of
S^{1}
:

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[12345|12345]=\,

\displaystyle\lambda^{3}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left(\!\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,(s_{12}\!-\!M_{12}^{2})(s_{34}\!-\!M_{34}^{2})}+\frac{1}{\,(s_{34}\!-\!M_{34}^{2})(s_{15}\!-\!M_{15}^{2})\,}+\frac{1}{\,(s_{15}\!-\!M_{15}^{2})(s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2})\,}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{(s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})}+\frac{1}{\,(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})(s_{12}\!-\!M_{12}^{2})\,}\!\right)\!,

(145a)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[13254|13254]=\,

\displaystyle\lambda^{3}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\left(\!\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,(s_{13}\!-\!M_{13}^{2})(s_{25}\!-\!M_{25}^{2})}+\frac{1}{\,(s_{25}\!-\!M_{25}^{2})(s_{14}\!-\!M_{14}^{2})\,}+\frac{1}{\,(s_{14}\!-\!M_{14}^{2})(s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2})\,}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{(s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})}+\frac{1}{\,(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})(s_{13}\!-\!M_{13}^{2})\,}\!\right)\!,

(145b)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[12345|13254]=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{3}}{\,(s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})\,}\,,

(145c)

where for simplicity we have suppressed the labels of KK numbers
for the external KK states and have defined the mass formula

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{ij}^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt(\mathfrak{n}_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{n}_{j})^{2}\!/\hskip-0.85358ptR^{2}\,
.

4.3
CHY Construction of Massive
KK Gauge/Gravity Amplitudes

In this subsection, we present the extended CHY construction of
the massive KK gauge boson amplitudes and KK graviton amplitudes
in the compactified 5d YM and GR theories. With these, we demonstrate the connection (equivalence) between
the massive CHY construction of the KK graviton amplitudes
and that of the massive BCJ double-copy
or the massive KLT relations at tree level.

According to the CHY formula (
125
),
we express the integrand
\,\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{N}^{\mathrm{YM}}\,

in the massless 5d YM theory and the integrand

\,\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{N}^{\mathrm{GR}}\,

in the massless 5d GR theory as follows:

\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{N}^{\mathrm{YM}}=\sum_{\{\alpha\}}\mathcal{C}[\alpha]\text{PT}[\alpha]\hskip 0.85358pt\text{Pf}^{\prime}(\widehat{\Psi}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN})\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 20.00003pt\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{N}^{\mathrm{GR}}=\mathrm{Pf}^{\prime}(\widehat{\Psi}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN})\hskip 0.85358pt\text{Pf}^{\prime}(\widehat{\Psi}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN})\hskip 0.85358pt,

(146)

where
\mathrm{Pf}^{\prime}(\widehat{\Psi}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN})
is the
reduced Pfaffian of a
2N\!\times\hskip-1.42262pt2N

anti-symmetric matrix consisting of four
N\!\times\!N

building blocks:

\mathrm{Pf}^{\prime}(\widehat{\Psi}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN})=\frac{~(-)^{i+j}~}{z_{i}\!-\!z_{j}}\mathrm{Pf}(\widehat{\Psi}^{ij}_{N,ij})\hskip 0.85358pt.

(147)

The definition of Pfaffian
Pf
is given by

\text{Pf}(X)\hskip 0.85358pt=\,\sum_{\alpha}\mathrm{sign}(\alpha)X_{\alpha(1)\alpha(2)}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdots\hskip-0.85358ptX_{\alpha(N-1)\alpha(N)}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(148)

where
X
is an arbitrary
N\!\times\!N
matrix,

\{\alpha\}
denotes all the possible ways to decompose

\{1,\cdots\hskip-1.42262pt,N\}
into pairs of indices, and

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{sign}(\alpha)\hskip 0.85358pt
equals to
+1
for even permutations
and equals
-1
otherwise. In addition,
\widehat{\Psi}^{ij}_{Nij}
is a

2(N\!\!-\!\!1)\!\hskip-0.85358pt\times\!2(N\!\!-\!\!1)
matrix,
so we can also express

\text{Pf}(\widehat{\Psi}^{ij}_{N,ij})\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\!\sqrt{\det(\widehat{\Psi}^{ij}_{N,ij})\,}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Under the 5d toroidal compactification, we derive the extended
anti-symmetric matrix
\widehat{\Psi}_{N}

with four
N\!\times\!N
blocks in the following form:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}

\displaystyle=\left(\!\begin{array}[]{cc}A_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}&-C^{\mathrm{T}}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}\\[2.84526pt]
C_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}&B_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}\end{array}\!\right)\!,\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle(A_{\hskip-0.85358ptN})_{ab}=\left\{\!\begin{aligned} \frac{~p_{a}\!\cdot p_{b}\!+\!M_{ab}^{2}~}{z_{a}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptz_{b}}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\hskip 11.38109pt(a\neq b),\\[2.84526pt]
0\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 24.18483pt&\hskip 11.38109pt(a=b),\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle(B_{\hskip-0.85358ptN})_{ab}

\displaystyle=\left\{\!\!\begin{aligned} \frac{\zeta_{a}\!\cdot\zeta_{b}}{~z_{a}\!-\!z_{b}~}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\hskip 11.38109pt(a\neq b),\\[2.84526pt]
0\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 15.649pt&\hskip 11.38109pt(a=b),\end{aligned}\right.\hskip 28.45274pt

\displaystyle(C_{\hskip-0.85358ptN})_{ab}=\left\{\!\begin{aligned} \frac{\zeta_{a}\!\cdot p_{b}}{~z_{a}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptz_{b}~}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 11.38109pt&\hskip 17.07164pt(a\neq b),\\[1.42262pt]
\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c=1\\
c\neq a\end{subarray}}^{N}\!\frac{~\zeta_{a}\!\cdot p_{c}\,}{~z_{a}\!-\hskip-0.85358ptz_{c}~}\hskip 0.85358pt,&\hskip 17.07164pt(a=b).\end{aligned}\right.

(149c)

In the formula of
A_{N}
, the squared-mass

\,M_{ab}^{2}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt{\mathfrak{n}_{a}\mathfrak{n}_{b}}/\hskip-0.85358pt{R^{2}}\hskip 0.85358pt

corresponds to the product of discretized 5th components
of the 5-dimensional momenta as in Eq.(
123
). For the case of
N\!=\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
, we can explicitly solve the
KK scattering equations
and compute the integrands via Eq.(
125
), where

(z_{i},z_{j},z_{k})\!=\!(0,1,\infty)

are fixed on the Riemann sphere. With these we can derive the four-point scattering amplitudes of
the KK gauge bosons and of the KK gravitons,
which agree with the results of Appendix
C

and Ref.
KKString
.

It is shown
CHY
that in the massless
scalar, gauge and gravity theories
the reduced Pfaffian can be decomposed into a sum of
products of the PT factor and the kinematic factor:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pf}^{\prime}(\Psi)

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{\alpha\hskip 0.85358pt\in S_{N-2}}\!\!\!\text{PT}[\alpha]\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[\alpha]\,.

(150)

The factors
\mathcal{N}[\alpha]
denote the kinematic numerators
which are functions of the momenta and polarization vectors and
can be calculated by labeled tree method
Gao:2017dek
. In the compactified massive KK theories,
each
\mathcal{N}[\alpha]
should be replaced by

{\mathcal{N}}[\alpha,\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]
which also depends on
KK indices (KK masses) of the external states, and can be
deduced from
\mathcal{N}[\alpha]
under the shifting

\hskip 0.85358pts_{ij}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!s_{ij}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-1.42262pt(\mathfrak{n}_{i}\!+\mathfrak{n}_{j})^{2}\!/R^{2}
,
as shown in Eq.(
12
).

Thus, using Eqs.(
146
), (
150
) and
(
132
),
we can derive the following BCJ-type double-copy construction
of the KK graviton amplitude from
the product of KK gauge boson amplitudes including
a kernel of the KK BAS amplitude:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\!\sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{C}[\alpha]\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\}\big)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}[\beta]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(151a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{YM}}_{N}

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\!\sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[\alpha,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}[\beta]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(151b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{GR}}_{N}

\displaystyle\,=\,\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\!\sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[\alpha,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[\beta,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(151c)

where
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in S_{N-2}
and for simplicity we have
suppressed the helicity indices and polarizations as well as
a conversion factor of the gauge-gravity coupling constants
in the last formula. The KK BAS kernel is defined as

\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big)=\int\!\!\text{d}\mu_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt\text{PT}[\alpha]\hskip 0.85358pt\text{PT}[\beta]\hskip 0.85358pt.

(152)

In Eq.(
151
), the BAS kernel
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{N}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}\hskip-0.85358pt\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big)

can be viewed as an
(N\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2)!\times\hskip-0.85358pt(N\!-2)!
matrix
and we denotes its inverse matrix as

\mathcal{K}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big)
. Then, inserting a unit matrix

\hskip 0.85358ptI\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt\sum_{\{\gamma\}}\!\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{N}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}\hskip-0.85358pt\big([\alpha|\gamma],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big)\mathcal{K}\big([\gamma|\beta],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big)\hskip 0.85358pt

into Eq.(
151c
), we can derive the following
double-copy relation between the KK graviton amplitude
and the products of the KK gauge boson amplitudes:

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{GR}}_{N}\,=\,\sum_{\mathsf{P}}\!\!\!\sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}}\!\!\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{YM}}_{N}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\alpha,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big)\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{YM}}_{N}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\beta,\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(153)

where the color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitude

\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{YM}}_{N}\big[\alpha,\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]
is given by
9
9

9

For notational simplicity, we will remove the extra superscripts
“YM” and “GR” on the amplitudes
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{M}
in the following
text after Eq.(
154
).

\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{YM}}_{N}\big[\alpha,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt=\,\sum_{\{\gamma\}}\mathcal{N}\big[\gamma,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}\big([\gamma|\alpha],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(154)

Comparing the above Eq.(
153
) with
Eq.(
128
) in Section
4.1

and keeping in mind the suppressed helicity indices
and polarizations mentioned below Eq.(
151
),
we see that the KK double-copy relation
(
153
) just reproduces the
extended massive KLT-type relation (
128
)
with the inverse matrix

\mathcal{K}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big)

of Eq.(
153
)
identified as the KLT kernel. In this way, the derivation of Eq.(
153
)
has proven
the interpretation of the kernel

\,\mathcal{K}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big)

on the right-hand side of Eq.(
128
)
as the inverse of the color-ordered KK BAS amplitude

\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}\big([\alpha|\beta],\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\hskip-0.85358pt\big)

in Eq.(
151a
).

In the following, we consider the four-point KK scattering amplitudes
to demonstrate explicitly how
Eqs.(
151a
)-(
151c
)
are realized. From (
151a
), we compute the four-point scattering
amplitude of KK bi-adjoint scalars:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{BAS}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathcal{C}_{s}~\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\big)\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{4}\!\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{s}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{u}\end{pmatrix}=\,-\!\sum_{j}\!\frac{\mathcal{C}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}}{~s_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}^{2}~}\,,

(155a)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{1}{\,s-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}^{2}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\,}~~&-\frac{1}{\,t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}\,}^{2}}\\[8.53581pt]
-\frac{1}{\,t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\,}\!~~&-\frac{1}{\,t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}\,}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,u-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}^{2}\,}\!\end{pmatrix}\!,

(155b)

where
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{s}\!=\!\mathcal{C}[1234]\,
and

\,\mathcal{C}_{u}\!=\!-\mathcal{C}[1324]\,

according to Eq.(
135
),
and the color factors
\{\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\prime}\}
(
\hskip 0.85358ptj\!=\!s,t,u
)
are obtained from
\{\mathcal{C}_{j}\}
by replacing the
hidden color indices

(a,b,c,d)\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!\hskip-0.85358pt(a^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358ptb^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358ptc^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358ptd^{\prime})\hskip 0.85358pt
. Comparing the above Eq.(
155
) with Eq.(
48
),
we find that the BAS amplitude kernel

\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{4}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\Theta\hskip 0.85358pt
. Hence, the BAS kernel
\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt
should
have the minimal rank of
(N\!-\hskip-0.85358pt3)!\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt

for
\hskip 0.85358ptN\!=\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
,
which leads to

\hskip 0.85358pt\det\!\big(\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}\big)\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt

and thus the same mass spectral condition (
52
).

There are correspondences between the kinematic numerators,

\mathcal{N}[1234,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]=\mathcal{N}_{s}\,,\hskip 20.00003pt\mathcal{N}[1324,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]=-\mathcal{N}_{u}\,,

(156)

which are analogous to the definition of their color counterparts
in Eq.(
135
). Using the relations (
156
) and Eq.(
151b
),
we derive the BCJ-type four-point massive KK gauge boson
scattering amplitude as follows:

\mathcal{T}_{4}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt\big(\mathcal{N}_{s}~\,\mathcal{N}_{u}\big)\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{4}\!\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{s}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{u}\end{pmatrix}=-\!\sum_{j}\!\frac{\mathcal{N}_{j}\hskip 1.42262pt\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}\,}{~s_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}^{2}~}\,,

(157)

where we have suppressed an overall coefficient
including the gauge coupling factor as indicated
below Eq.(
151
). In Eq.(
157
) and hereafter, for notational simplicity
we have removed the superscript “YM” on the color-ordered
KK gauge boson amplitude (
154
). Then, substituting Eq.(
154
) into Eq.(
151b
),
we can express the
N
-point color-dressed KK gauge boson amplitude
in terms of the color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitudes:

\mathcal{T}_{4}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]=\,\sum_{\{\alpha\}}\mathcal{C}[\alpha]\mathcal{T}_{N}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\alpha,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]]\,.

(158)

For the
N\!=\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
case, we derive the following explicit
relations to decompose the four-point color-dressed KK gauge boson
amplitude into the color-ordered KK amplitudes:

\mathcal{T}_{4}\hskip-0.85358pt\big[\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]=\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{C}_{s}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{4}\big[1234,\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]-\mathcal{C}_{u}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{4}\big[1324,\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]\hskip 0.85358pt,

(159)

where each color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitude is
gauge-invariant separately. Next, with Eq.(
156
)
we substitute the KK BAS kernel matrix (
155b
)
into Eq.(
151c
) and derive the following BCJ-type
double-copy construction of the four-point KK graviton
scattering amplitudes:

\mathcal{M}_{4}\big[\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]=\hskip 0.85358pt\big(\mathcal{N}_{s}~\,\mathcal{N}_{u}\big)\hskip 0.85358pt\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{4}\!\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{N}_{s}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathcal{N}_{u}\hskip-0.85358pt\end{pmatrix}=\,-\!\sum_{j}\!\frac{~\mathcal{N}_{j}(\lambda)\mathcal{N}_{j}(\lambda^{\prime})~}{~s_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{j}}^{2}~}\,,

(160)

where
\hskip 0.85358ptj\!\in\!\{s,t,u\}\hskip 0.85358pt
and we have suppressed the overall
coupling coefficient as well as possible summations of the
external KK gauge boson polarizations
[with helicity indices indicated by
(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})
]
on the right-hand side according to the definition of
the KK graviton polarization tensors
(
25
).

We further note that the kernel in Eq.(
153
) is a

(N\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2)!\hskip-1.42262pt\times\!(N\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2)!
matrix,
while the kernel in Eq.(
128
) is a

(N\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-1.42262pt3)!\hskip-0.85358pt\times\!(N\!-\hskip-0.85358pt3)!\hskip 0.85358pt
matrix. Thus, we will eliminate the redundant variables
by making use of the extended fundamental BCJ relations
for KK gauge boson amplitudes. As an example, according to Eq.(
151c
), the four-point
KK graviton scattering amplitude can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{4}\big[\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}\big]

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[1234,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{4}[1234,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]+\mathcal{N}[1324,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{4}[1324,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]

\displaystyle=\left(\!\mathcal{N}[1234,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]+\frac{\,s\!-\!M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{s}}^{2}\,}{\,u\!-\!M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{u}}^{2}\,}\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{N}[1324,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\!\right)\!\mathcal{T}_{4}[1234,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]

\displaystyle=\hskip 0.85358pt(\hskip 0.85358ptt\!-\!M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2})\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{4}[1234,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{T}_{4}[1324,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\,,

(161)

where we have denoted the color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitudes

\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathcal{T}_{4}}[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}]\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt{\mathcal{T}_{4}}[1234,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\,
and

\hskip 0.85358pt{\mathcal{T}_{4}}[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}]\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt{\mathcal{T}_{4}}[1324,\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and have suppressed an overall factor

\hskip 0.85358pt\kappa^{2}/16\hskip 0.85358pt
as well as possible summations of the
external KK gauge boson polarizations on the right-hand side
according to the definition of the KK graviton polarization tensors
(
25
). In the second line of Eq.(
4.3
),
we have used the extended fundamental BCJ relation
(
49
) and the
correspondences of kinematic numerators in Eq.(
156
). The above formula (
4.3
)
gives an extended KLT-like relation between the four-point KK gaviton
scattering amplitude and product of the two corresponding color-ordered
KK gauge boson amplitudes. Moreover, we note that the kernel

\hskip 0.85358ptt\!-\!M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}

is given by the inverse of massive double color-ordered amplitude
of the KK bi-adjoint scalars:

\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{S}}[1234|1324;\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\}]=\frac{1}{~t-M_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{n}_{t}}^{2}~}\,,

(162)

which can be derived based on the analysis in Sec.
4.2

or given by the off-diagonal element
\{12\}
of the

\hskip 0.85358pt\Theta\hskip 0.85358pt
matrix in Eq.(
48
). Then, comparing Eq.(
4.3
) with Eq.(
47
),
we see that Eq.(
4.3
) gives the same form
as the four-point massive KLT relation (
47
)
except that Eq.(
47
) has made a special choice of
the external KK states to have longitudinal polarizations and
Eq.(
4.3
) has suppressed an overall factor and
the possible summations of the external KK gauge boson polarizations on
its right-hand side [as mentioned below Eq.(
4.3
)].

According to the above massive KK CHY formulation,
the KK graviton scattering amplitudes (
128
)
can be constructed by inputting the kernel

\mathcal{K}\!\left([\alpha|\beta],\!\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\}\hskip-0.85358pt\right)

(as provided by the inverse of the KK BAS amplitudes given by
Sec.
4.2
) and the color-ordered KK gauge boson
amplitudes
\mathcal{T}_{N}\big[\alpha,\hskip-0.85358pt\{\mathfrak{n}_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\}\big]

[as further expressed in Eq.(
154
)]. For the four-point scattering, there is one independent
color-ordering of the KK gauge boson amplitudes after applying
the massive fundamental BCJ relation (
49
). For instance, we may choose the color-ordering [1234] and
derive the corresponding massive KK gauge boson amplitude
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}_{4}}\big[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}\big]\hskip-0.85358pt\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358ptg^{2}\!\left(\!\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{1}}{~s_{12}\!-\!M_{12}^{2}~}+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{2}}{~s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2}~}\!\right)\!,

(163)

where
\hskip 0.85358pts_{12}\!=\!-(p_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptp_{2})^{2}\!=\!s\,

and
\hskip 0.85358pts_{23}\!=\!\hskip-0.85358pt-(p_{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptp_{3})^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!t\hskip 0.85358pt
. The above color-ordered numerators

(\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{1},\hskip 0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{2})

are expressed as

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{1}\!=

\displaystyle-2\hskip 0.85358pt(s_{23}\!-\!M^{2}_{23})[12][34]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt\Big\{\!(12)(13)[14]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(12)(23)[14]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(12)(31)[34]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(12)(34)[13]

\displaystyle-\hskip-0.85358pt(13)(21)[24]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(13)(24)[12]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(23)[24]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(32)[34]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(34)[23]

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt(23)(24)[12]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(23)(34)[12]\Big\}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(164a)

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{2}\!=

\displaystyle\,2\hskip 0.85358pt(s_{13}\!-\!M^{2}_{13})[14][23]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pt(s_{23}\!-\!M^{2}_{23})\Big\{\![12][34]\!-\![13][24]\!\Big\}\!-\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt\Big\{\!(12)(23)[14]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(13)(32)[14]

\displaystyle-\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(23)[24]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(32)[34]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(34)[23]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(23)(24)[12]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(23)(31)[24]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(23)(34)[12]

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt(24)(31)[23]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(24)(32)[13]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(32)(31)[34]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(32)(34)[13]\hskip-0.85358pt\Big\}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(164b)

In the above we have defined abbreviations

(ij)\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt(p_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\zeta_{j})
,

[ij]\hskip-0.85358pt\equiv\hskip-0.85358pt(\zeta_{i}\hskip-0.85358pt\cdot\zeta_{j})\hskip 0.85358pt
, and

\,\zeta_{j}^{\mu}\!\equiv\!\zeta_{\lambda_{j}}^{\mu}

to simplify the notations.

As another explicit example,
we take the five-point KK gauge boson amplitude
to show how the above massive KK CHY formulation works. The five-point gauge boson amplitudes have

\hskip 0.85358pt(5\hskip-0.85358pt-3)!\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip-0.85358pt2\,

independent color-orderings, which may be chosen as

[12345]
and
[13254]
. The five-point KK BAS amplitudes are studied
in Section
4.2
. We deduced the extended BCJ-type five-point KK gauge boson
amplitude in Section
3.4
. The five-point color-ordered massless
KK gauge boson amplitude can be calculated by the CHY formula. Then, we use the shifting method of Section
2.2

to derive the corresponding five-point massive KK amplitude
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}_{5}}\big[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}5^{\mathfrak{n}_{5}}_{\lambda_{5}}\big]\hskip-0.85358pt=g^{3}\!\left\{\!\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt1}}{\,(s_{12}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{12}^{2})(s_{34}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{34}^{2})\,}+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt2}}{\,(s_{34}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{34}^{2})(s_{15}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{15}^{2})\,}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left.+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt3}}{\,(s_{15}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{15}^{2})(s_{23}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{23}^{2})\,}+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt4}}{\,(s_{23}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{23}^{2})(s_{45}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{45}^{2})\,}+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt5}}{\,(s_{45}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{45}^{2})(s_{12}\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{12}^{2})\,}\!\right\}\!,

(165a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{5}\big[1^{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}_{\lambda_{1}}3^{\mathfrak{n}_{3}}_{\lambda_{3}}2^{\mathfrak{n}_{2}}_{\lambda_{2}}5^{\mathfrak{n}_{5}}_{\lambda_{5}}4^{\mathfrak{n}_{4}}_{\lambda_{4}}\big]=g^{3}\bigg\{\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}^{\prime}_{\hskip-1.42262pt1}}{\,(s_{13}\!-\!M_{13}^{2})(s_{25}\!-\!M_{25}^{2})}+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}^{\prime}_{\hskip-1.42262pt2}}{\,(s_{25}\!-\!M_{25}^{2})(s_{14}\!-\!M_{14}^{2})\,}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}^{\prime}_{\hskip-1.42262pt3}}{\,(s_{14}\!-\!M_{14}^{2})(s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2})\,}+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}^{\prime}_{\hskip-1.42262pt4}}{(s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2})(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})}+\frac{\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}^{\prime}_{\hskip-1.42262pt5}}{\,(s_{45}\!-\!M_{45}^{2})(s_{13}\!-\!M_{13}^{2})}\bigg\}\,.

(165b)

We note that in the above color-ordered KK gauge amplitudes
contain the product of the numerator

\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt{\ell}}

(or
\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}^{\prime}_{\hskip-1.42262pt{\ell}}\hskip 0.85358pt
)
and the fraction
\,1/[(\cdots)(\cdots)]\,
in each term,

or

\hskip 0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}^{\prime}_{\hskip-1.42262pt{\ell}}\hskip 0.85358pt

(
{\ell}\!=\!1,2,\cdots\!,5
)
in Eq.(

) by shifting the massless numerators
(computed previously
CHY
),

\,\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt{\ell}}\!=\hskip-1.42262pt{\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt\ell}^{\,\mathfrak{n}_{j}=0}\big|}_{\hat{s}_{ij}\rightarrow\hskip 0.85358pts_{ij}\hskip-0.85358pt-M_{ij}^{2}}\hskip 0.85358pt
