\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\hbox to0.0pt{\thinspace\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt1}=

\displaystyle\,2\hskip 0.85358pt\hskip-0.85358pt(s_{23}\!-\!M_{23}^{2})[12]\Big\{\hskip-0.85358pt[34](45)\!+\hskip-1.42262pt[35](14)\!+\hskip-1.42262pt[35](24)\!+\hskip-1.42262pt[35](34)\!-\hskip-1.42262pt[45](43)\!\Big\}

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pt(s_{24}\!-\!M_{24}^{2})[12][45]\Big\{\!(13)\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(23)\!\Big\}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt2\hskip 0.85358pt(s_{34}\!-\!M_{34}^{2})[45]\Big\{\![12](23)+[13](12)\!-\![23](21)\!\Big\}

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt4\hskip 0.85358pt\Big\{\!(12)(13)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(45)[14]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(41)[45]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(54)[15]\Big]\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(12)(14)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(23)[15]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(31)[35]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(35)[13]\hskip-0.85358pt\Big]

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt(12)(23)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(24)[15]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(34)[15]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(41)[45]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(45)[14]\Big]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(12)(24)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(31)[35]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(35)[13]\Big]

\displaystyle-\hskip-0.85358pt(12)(31)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(34)[35]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(43)[45]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(45)[34]\Big]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(12)(34)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[(35)[13]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(45)[13]\Big]

(166)

\displaystyle-\hskip-0.85358pt(13)(14)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[(21)[25]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(25)[12]\Big]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(13)(21)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(24)[25]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(34)[25]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(42)[45]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(45)[24]\Big]

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt(13)(24)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(25)[12]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(45)[12]\Big]\!\hskip-0.85358pt+\!(13)(25)(34)[12]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(14)(21)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(23)[25]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(32)[35]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(35)[23]\hskip-0.85358pt\Big]

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt(14)(23)[12]\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(25)\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(35)\Big]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(23)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(24)[25]\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(34)[25]\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(42)[45]\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358pt(45)[24]\Big]

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(24)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[(32)[35]\hskip-0.85358pt-\hskip-0.85358pt(35)[23]\Big]\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(32)\Big[(34)[35]\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(43)[45]\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(45)[34]\Big]

\displaystyle-\hskip-0.85358pt(21)(34)[23]\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-1.42262pt(35)\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-1.42262pt(45)\Big]\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt(23)[12]\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-0.85358pt(24)(15)\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-1.42262pt(25)(34)\Big]\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(23)(34)[12]\hskip-0.85358pt\Big[\hskip-1.42262pt(35)\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-1.42262pt(45)\hskip-0.85358pt\Big]\Big\},

where
(ij)\!\equiv\!(p_{i}\hskip-1.42262pt\cdot\hskip-0.85358pt\zeta_{j})
,

[ij]\!\equiv\!(\zeta_{i}\hskip-1.42262pt\cdot\zeta_{j})
, and

\,\zeta_{j}^{\mu}\!\equiv\!\zeta_{\lambda_{j}}^{\mu}
. The other massive numerators

\hskip 0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}_{\hskip-1.42262pt{\ell}}\hskip 0.85358pt
and

\hskip 0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\>\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathcal{N}$}}^{\prime}_{\hskip-1.42262pt{\ell}}\hskip 0.85358pt

can be derived similarly. Thus, to construct the five-point KK graviton scattering amplitudes
from the extended massive CHY formula (
128
)
[or, Eq.(
153
)],
we can use the above color-ordered massive KK gauge boson amplitudes
and express the kernel of Eq.(
128
)
in terms of the inverse of the KK bi-adjoint scalar amplitudes
(

).

5
Determining KK Structure from Mass Spectral Condition

We proved in Section
3.1
that
under the toroidal compactification (without orbifold)
the four-point scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons
and KK gravitons satisfy a mass spectral condition
(
41
) [or (
52
)]. We may rewrite it in the following generic form:

M_{1}^{2}+M_{2}^{2}+M_{3}^{2}+M_{4}^{2}\,=\,M_{12}^{2}+M_{13}^{2}+M_{14}^{2}\,,

(167)

where
M_{j}
denotes the mass of each external state
and
(M_{12}^{2},M_{14}^{2},M_{13}^{2})
equal respectively the
squared pole-mass in each of the
(s,t,u)
channels. Due to the momentum conservation for the four-particle scattering,
we also have relations

M_{12}^{2}\!=\!M_{34}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
,

M_{13}^{2}\!=\!M_{24}^{2}
, and

M_{14}^{2}\!=\!M_{23}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. It is natural to ask: whether there exist any different theories
(other than such compactified KK theories) which could satisfy
this mass spectral condition?

We inspect Eq.(
167
) and find that it
implies three
necessary
conditions. The first one is that the massive theory should contain
at least two types of particles with unequal masses. This is because for a theory having only
one type of particles with the same mass
\hskip 0.85358ptM\hskip 0.85358pt
,
the two sides of Eq.(
167
) would become

\,4\hskip 0.85358ptM^{2}\!\neq\!3\hskip 0.85358ptM^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt

(based on the cubic interaction vertex to be defined below). The second condition is that there exists only a simple pole
in each of the
(s,t,u)
channels. The third condition is that the scattering amplitude should
include the contributions from all three
kinematic channels of
(s,t,u)
. The second condition means that, for a generic cubic vertex

V_{\textbf{abc}}
involving three particles

(\textbf{a},\textbf{b},\textbf{c})
,
the mass
\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\textbf{c}}\hskip 0.85358pt
of the third particle
c

is uniquely determined once we specify the particles

a
and
b
(having masses
\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\textbf{a}}\hskip 0.85358pt

and
M_{\textbf{b}}
respectively). In a given theory, we denote the particles of type

{\bf a}_{j}
if they all have the same mass

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\textbf{a}_{j}}
and same spin
\textsf{s}_{\textbf{a}_{j}}\hskip-0.85358pt
. Each type of particles (say, type
{\bf a}_{j}
)
can be viewed as a set of states (having the same mass and spin)
in Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}\hskip 0.85358pt
. For a given cubic vertex

V_{\textbf{abc}}
, once the states
a
and
b
are chosen,
then the state
c
in the vertex
V_{\textbf{abc}}
is also fixed
due to conservations of the momentum and angular momentum. Hence, if a given four-point scattering amplitude
has single-pole structure in each kinematic channel,
we can always realize such an
amplitude by using the cubic vertex
V_{\textbf{abc}}

where once the states
a
and
b
are chosen as the
external states, then the third state
c
is uniquely determined. Applying such cubic interactions of
V_{\textbf{abc}}

to the states in the Hilbert space,
we have the following operation
\mathcal{F}\,
:

\mathcal{F}(\textbf{a},\textbf{b})~\mapsto~\textbf{c}\,,

(168)

where
\,\textbf{a},\textbf{b},\textbf{c}\!\in\!\mathcal{H}\,
. For instance, in the 4d massless QCD the cubic gluon vertex provides
the simplest example of the operation (
168
),
where the three gluons in the cubic vertex have different color indices
due to the anti-symmetric structure constant
f^{abc}
of the gauge
group. In this case, the four-point massless gluon
scattering amplitudes obey the mass-spectral condition
(
167
) trivially.
10
10

10

In the Yang-Mills type of non-Abelian gauge theories, there also exist
quartic gauge interaction vertices, which contribute to the four-point
contact diagrams. But the contributions of such contact diagrams can
be “blown up” into the
(s,t,u)
channel pole diagrams (induced by
cubic vertices)
BCJ:2008
BCJ:2019
. Hence, for our current analysis only the cubic
interactions (denoted by
V_{\textbf{abc}}
) need to be concerned. From the BCJ-type double-copy constructions, the GR type of four-point
graviton amplitudes will exhibit similar structures with the

(s,t,u)
channel pole diagrams (induced by cubic gravitational vertices). It is known that all the nonlinear gravitational self-interactions
can be induced precisely as having
cubic graviton interactions
Deser
.

Then, we denote a set of particles (having the same mass
M_{j}

and joining the same type of cubic interactions

V_{\textbf{abc}}\hskip 0.85358pt
) by
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt
. All the particles in the given theory can form a set denoted as

\mathbb{P}
which is divided into the smaller sub-sets
in terms of their masses and types of interactions:

\mathbb{P}\,=\,\mathfrak{M}_{0}\cup\mathfrak{M}_{1}\cup\mathfrak{M}_{2}\cup\cdots\,.

(169)

Then we can define a new group

\mathcal{G}=\{\mathfrak{M}_{0},\mathfrak{M}_{1},\mathfrak{M}_{2},\cdots\}
,
where each sub-set
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt

serves as a group element. We may use
\mathfrak{M}_{0}
to denote the set
collecting the massless particles such as the massless gravitons
or gauge bosons, which can be further divided into several
subsets
\mathfrak{M}_{0\texttt{s}}

according to the different spin-
s

of each type of the massless particles. Formally, we can define a multiplicative operation

\hskip 0.85358pt*\hskip 0.85358pt
for the elements of
\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
:

\mathfrak{M}_{ij}\equiv\mathfrak{M}_{i}*\mathfrak{M}_{j}~\mapsto~\mathfrak{M}_{k}\,,

(170)

where
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{i},\mathfrak{M}_{j},\mathfrak{M}_{k}\!\in\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
. This means that for any states
\textbf{a}\!\in\!\mathfrak{M}_{i}

and
\textbf{b}\!\in\!\mathfrak{M}_{j}
, the above multiplicative operation
(
170
)
is realized by the cubic interaction vertex

V_{\textbf{abc}}
, namely,

\,\textbf{a}*\textbf{b}\mapsto\textbf{c}\,
with

\hskip 0.85358pt\textbf{c}\hskip-0.85358pt\in\hskip-0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{k}\hskip 0.85358pt
. The crossing symmetry of the scattering amplitudes guarantees

\,\mathfrak{M}_{ij}\!=\mathfrak{M}_{ji}\,
,
which realizes the commutative law of multiplication. We also note that all the particles in a given set

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{i}\!\in\!\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt

share the same mass
\hskip 0.85358ptm_{i}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
but different types of particles in different sets may
have same masses. For instance, the KK gluons and KK gravitons
of the same KK-level
n
can belong to two different sets of

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{n_{i}}
and
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{n_{j}}
.

The second hidden property implied by the mass-spectral condition
(
167
) is the existence of all the three
kinematic channels
(s,t,u)
. This ensures the following
associative law of multiplication for the group
\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
:

(\mathfrak{M}_{i}*\mathfrak{M}_{j})*\mathfrak{M}_{k}=(\mathfrak{M}_{k}*\mathfrak{M}_{i})*\mathfrak{M}_{j}=(\mathfrak{M}_{j}*\mathfrak{M}_{k})*\mathfrak{M}_{i}\,.

(171)

Hence the multiplication
\hskip 0.85358pt*\hskip 0.85358pt

is an associative binary operation on the group
\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

We note that the massless gravitons can form a set
(as denoted by
\mathfrak{M}_{0h}\!=\!\{h^{\mu\nu}\}
)
and deserve a special attention. In general, any given QFT model can naturally contain
massless gravitons or be combined with the GR (in the
effective field theory formulation
EFT
)
because gravitation is universal and the
massless gravitons of the GR interact with all partices living
in the spacetime. For the generic graviton-matter coupling via

h_{\mu\nu}T_{i}^{\mu\nu}
with
T^{\mu\nu}
being the
energy-momentum tensor, it always conatins the cubic vertex

\,h_{\mu\nu}
-
\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{i}\hskip 0.85358pt
-
\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{i}\,
,
where
\phi_{i}
denotes any matter field in the theory. Consider, for instance, the scattering amplitude of

\hskip 0.85358pth\,\phi_{1}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!\phi_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{3}\,

at tree level, where the intermediate particle can only be one of

(\phi_{1},\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{2},\hskip 0.85358pt\phi_{3})
for each channel of

(s,t,u)
. As we can readily check, this scattering amplitude does obey
the mass spectral condition (
167
):
\,0+M_{1}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{2}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{3}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt=\!M_{1}^{2}\!+\!M_{2}^{2}\!+\!M_{3}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Moreover, it is clear that the tree-level
four-point massless graviton scattering amplitude

\mathcal{M}[h_{\sigma_{1}}h_{\sigma_{2}}h_{\sigma_{3}}h_{\sigma_{4}}]

also satisfies the condition (
167
)
because all the external and internal graviton states are massless. Then, we consider a more general theory containing additional
massive gravitons and massive gauge bosons. Hence, for the set

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{0h}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\{h^{\mu\nu}\}

of massless gravitons,
we can use the cubic vertex

\,V_{3}(h^{\mu\nu}\hskip-0.85358pt\phi_{i}\phi_{i})\,

to define the multiplicative operations on the group
\mathcal{G}\,
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0h}*\hskip 0.85358pt\mathfrak{M}_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt=\,\mathfrak{M}_{i}\,,

(172a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{i}*\hskip 0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathfrak{M}$}}_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.85358pt=\,\mathfrak{M}_{0h}\,,

(172b)

where in the second equation

\hskip 0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathfrak{M}$}}_{i}\!=\!\{\bar{\phi}_{i}\}

denotes the set of anti-particles
which have the same mass
\hskip 0.85358ptm_{i}
as those particles in
the set
\mathfrak{M}_{i}\!=\!\{\phi_{i}\}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, the set
\mathfrak{M}_{0h}
serves as the identity element
of the group
\mathcal{G}
. In the case that the particles

\phi_{j}\!\hskip-0.85358pt\in\!\mathfrak{M}_{j}

are real fields and carry no charge, we have

\,\mathfrak{M}_{j}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hskip 4.99998pt\hbox{$\overline{\hbox{}}$}\hss}{\hbox{$\mathfrak{M}$}}_{j}\hskip 0.85358pt
. In general, because the commutative law of multiplication
requires
\,\mathfrak{M}_{ij}\!=\mathfrak{M}_{ji}\,

as discussed below Eq.(
170
),
we deduce that the group
\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
forms
a Abelian group and
*
is the multiplicative operation
defined for its elements. Moreover, in a physical system
all the particle masses should be described
by a finite number of physical parameters. It means that this Abelian group
\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt

should be a finitely generated Abelian group
abelian-group

and thus has a finite number of generating elements (generators)
which compose the generating set of
\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

The fundamental theorem of the finitely generated Abelian group
states
abelian-group
that
every finitely generated Abelian group is isomorphic to a
direct sum of the primary cyclic groups (
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hskip 0.85358pt
)
and the free Abelian group (containing copies of the infinite
cyclic group which is just the integer group
\mathbb{Z}\hskip 0.85358pt
). Each primary cyclic group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
contains natural numbers
modulo
\hskip 0.85358ptp\hskip 0.85358pt
, i.e.,

\mathbb{Z}_{p}\!=\!\{0,1,2,\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,p\!-\!1\}
,
with
p
being a prime number or a power of prime number.
11
11

11

The cyclic group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is isomorphic to the Gaussian residue class
group
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}/p\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}\hskip 0.85358pt

abelian-group
.
Thus, the finitely generated Abelian group
\mathcal{G}

can be decomposed as follows:

\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt\cong\,\mathbb{Z}^{r}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p_{1}}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p_{2}}\!\oplus\!\cdots\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p_{s}},\hskip 28.45274pt(\hskip 0.85358ptr,s\!\in\!\mathbb{N}\hskip 0.85358pt),

(173)

where the natural number
\hskip 0.85358ptr\hskip 0.85358pt
is the rank of
group
\mathcal{G}
and
(p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{s})

are powers of (not necessarily distinct) prime numbers. The values of
(r,\hskip 0.85358ptp_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptp_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358ptp_{s})

are uniquely determined by
\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
. The primary cyclic groups

(\mathbb{Z}_{p_{1}},\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}_{p_{2}},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\mathbb{Z}_{p_{s}})

have finite orders

(p_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptp_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358ptp_{s})

which are the torsion coefficients of
\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
.
Hence, we can assign any particle in this theory by
a set of integers
\{n_{1},n_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,n_{r+s}\}

(which belong to the cyclic groups of
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{G}\hskip 0.85358pt
respectively)
to characterize its mass

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{n_{1},\hskip 1.42262ptn_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 1.42262ptn_{r+s}\}}
,
where each integer
\hskip 0.85358ptn_{k}\hskip 0.85358pt
is additively conserved mod

p_{k}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, the identity elements

\{n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,n_{r+s}\}\!=\!\{0,\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 0.85358pt0\}

should correspond to the mass

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{0,\hskip 0.85358pt\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 1.42262pt0\}}\!=0\hskip 0.85358pt
. For the convenience of demonstration, let us consider
for instance a nontrivial cyclic group
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}_{n}

(with
n\!\geqslant\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
)
in Eq.(
173
). With the above formulation, we can compute both sides of the
mass spectral condition (
167
) for the
external particles having indices

\{n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},n_{3},n_{4}\}\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt\{1,\hskip 0.85358ptn\!-\!1,\hskip 0.85358pt1,\hskip 0.85358ptn\!-\!1\}

and obtain the following:

M_{\{1\}}^{2}+M_{\{n-1\}}^{2}+M_{\{1\}}^{2}+M_{\{n-1\}}^{2}=\,0+0+M_{\{2\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(174)

where on the right-hand side of the equality
we have used the fact of

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{n\}}\!\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{\{0\}}\!\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt

because the elements of
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}_{n}
are mod
\hskip 0.85358ptn\hskip 0.85358pt

and thus
\hskip 0.85358pt\{n\}\!=\!\{0\}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Moreover, applying the relation

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{1\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\{n-1\}}^{2}

to Eq.(
180
),
we deduce from Eq.(
180
) a condition

\,M_{\{2\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!4\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{1\}}^{2}
.
Then, we will use induction method to prove the mass formula

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{k\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\hskip-0.85358ptk^{2}M_{\{1\}}^{2}
. This formula obviously holds for
k=0,1\hskip 0.85358pt
, and we already explicitly verified that it holds for
k\!=\!2

as shown above. Let us suppose that the formula

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{\ell\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\ell^{2}M_{\{1\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt

holds for
\hskip 0.85358pt\ell\hskip-0.85358pt\leqslant\hskip-0.85358ptk\hskip 0.85358pt
. Then, the remaining task is to prove that
this formula can hold for
\hskip 0.85358pt\ell\!=\!k\hskip-0.85358pt+\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, we further consider a choice of the mass indices of
the external particles

\{n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},n_{3},n_{4}\}\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt\{1,\hskip 0.85358ptk,\hskip 0.85358ptn\!-\!1,\hskip 0.85358ptn\!-\!k\}
,
and compute both sides of the spectral condition
(
167
) as follows:

M_{\{1\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+M_{\{k\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+M_{\{n-1\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+M_{\{n-k\}}^{2}=\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{1+k\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+M_{\{1-k\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+0\,.

(175)

Because these mass indices are additively conserved mod
n
of

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, we can simplify Eq.(
175
) as,

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{k+1\}}^{2}\!=\!2\hskip 0.85358pt\big(M_{\{k\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\{1\}}^{2}\big)\hskip-1.42262pt-\!M_{\{k-1\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Here we have made use of the fact that a particle and its
anti-particle must share the same mass, leading to

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{k\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!M_{\{-k\}}^{2}
for any integer
k\hskip 0.85358pt
. Using the formula

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{\ell\}}^{2}\!\!=\!\hskip-0.85358pt\ell^{2}M_{\{1\}}^{2}

for
\hskip 0.85358pt\ell\!\leqslant\!k\hskip 0.85358pt
, we can thus deduce:
M_{\{k+1\}}^{2}\!\!=\!(k\!+\!1)^{2}M_{\{1\}}^{2}
. This completes our proof by induction, and we can conclude:

M_{\{k\}}^{2}=\hskip 0.85358ptk^{2}M_{\{1\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 22.76219pt(\hskip 0.85358pt\text{for}~0\leqslant k\leqslant n\hskip-0.85358pt-\!1\hskip 0.85358pt)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(176)

But we must have the relation

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{n-1\}}^{2}\!\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{\{1\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt

because the mass indices are additively conserved mod
n
of

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}_{n}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Together with Eq.(
176
) for

\hskip 0.85358ptk\!=\!n\!-\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
, this leads to the condition:

n(n\!-\!2)\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{1\}}^{2}=\,0\,.

(177)

It has a nontrivial solution
\hskip 0.85358ptn\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!2\hskip 1.42262pt
,
or, we can avoid this condition (
177
) altogether
by removing the constraint of the cyclic relation

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{n-1\}}^{2}\!\!=\hskip-0.85358pt\!M_{\{1\}}^{2}

which could happen
only if

\hskip 0.85358ptn\!=\!\infty\hskip 0.85358pt
. Because of
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}\!\!\subset\!\mathbb{Z}\hskip 1.42262pt
,
the case of
\,\mathbb{Z}_{n}\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!\mathbb{Z}_{\infty}\hskip 0.85358pt
just goes back to
a subgroup of the integer groups
\mathbb{Z}^{r}

(the free Abelian group) of the finitely generated group

\mathcal{G}
as defined in Eq.(
173
).

In the following we will first check the solution

\hskip 0.85358ptn\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!2\hskip 1.42262pt
and show that it is excluded by
the mass spectral condition (
167
). For this, using the group
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
, we construct the simplest
realization including the massless graviton field
h^{\mu\nu}

and a massive real field
\hskip 0.85358pt\phi\hskip 0.85358pt

(which has no self-interaction and describes a type of
particles equal to their own anti-particles). The field
\phi
only interacts with the graviton field
h^{\mu\nu}

through the cubic vertex of
\phi
-
\phi
-
h\hskip 0.85358pt
,
denoted as
V_{3}(h\hskip 0.85358pt\phi\hskip 0.85358pt\phi)
. For example,
\phi
can be a real massive scalar field, or a
massive photon, or a massive Majorana fermion. This model contains two types of cubic interaction vertices

\phi
-
\phi
-
h
and
h
-
h
-
h\hskip 0.85358pt
. We present the multiplication table of this model
on the left, in comparison
with the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
multiplication table on the right:

V_{3}(h\hskip 0.85358pt\phi\hskip 0.85358pt\phi)

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h^{\mu\nu}

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\phi

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h^{\mu\nu}

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}h^{\mu\nu}

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\phi

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\phi

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\phi

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}h^{\mu\nu}

\mathbb{Z}_{2}

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}0

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}1

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}0

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}0

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}1

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}1

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}1

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}0

(178)

The above tables demonstrate that the defined operations for the

\{\phi,\hskip 0.85358pth^{\mu\nu}\}
model do satisfy the multiplication rule
(addition) of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
group. Consider for instance the scattering amplitude of

\,\phi\,\phi\!\rightarrow\!\phi\,\phi\,
,
which is contributed by exchanging
the massless graviton fields
h^{\mu\nu}
. It is clear that this process violates
the mass spectral condition (
167
)
unless
\hskip 0.85358pt\phi\hskip 0.85358pt
is also set as massless which
however becomes a trivial case. Thus, the condition (
167
)
excludes
\hskip 0.85358ptn\!=\hskip-0.85358pt2\hskip 1.42262pt
as a nontrivial solution
of the physical massive theory. Hence, we conclude that

the integer groups
\mathbb{Z}^{r}
give the unique realization of the
mass spectral condition (
167
).

Next, taking one of the integer group
\mathbb{Z}
of
\mathbb{Z}^{r}
,
we will prove that the mass spectral condition (
167
)
has the unique solution:

M_{\{k\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358ptk^{2}M_{\{1\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,\hskip 22.76219pt(\hskip 0.85358pt\text{for}~k\in\mathbb{Z}\hskip 0.85358pt)\hskip 0.85358pt.

(179)

This can be proven by induction method. Choosing the indices of the external states as

\{n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},n_{3},n_{4}\}\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt\{0,\hskip 0.85358pt0,\hskip 0.85358pt0,\hskip 0.85358pt0\}
, we deduce
from the spectral condition (
167
):
4M_{\{0\}}^{2}\!\!=\!3M_{\{0\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
and thus

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{0\}}\!\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
.
Then, choosing
\{n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},n_{3},n_{4}\}\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt\{n,\hskip 0.85358pt-n,\hskip 0.85358pt0,\hskip 0.85358pt0\}
and using

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{0\}}\!=\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
, we deduce from the condition
(
167
):
M_{\{-n\}}^{2}\!=\!M_{\{n\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. We further choose
\{n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},n_{3},n_{4}\}\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt\{n,\hskip 0.85358ptn,\hskip 0.85358pt-n,\hskip 0.85358pt-n\}

and deduce from the condition
(
167
):
M_{\{2n\}}^{2}\!\!=\!4M_{\{n\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt

and thus
\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{2\}}^{2}\!\!=\!4M_{\{1\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. For induction proof, we suppose that Eq.(
179
) holds
for
\hskip 0.85358ptk\!=\!n\hskip 0.85358pt
. Then, choosing
\{n_{1},\hskip 0.85358ptn_{2},n_{3},n_{4}\}\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt\{n,\hskip 0.85358pt-n,\hskip 0.85358pt1,\hskip 0.85358pt-1\}
, we
deduce from the condition (
167
):

M_{\{n\}}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\{-n\}}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\{1\}}^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\{-1\}}^{2}=\,0+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\{n+1\}}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{\{n-1\}}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(180)

which can be further simplified as

M_{\{n+1\}}^{2}\!\!=\!(n\!+\!1)^{2}M_{\{1\}}^{2}
. This completes our proof of the mass formula (
179
)
by induction. Since the physical mass
M_{\{k\}}
should be defined
as nonnegative, we can solve Eq.(
179
) as follows:

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{k\}}\!=|\widetilde{M}_{\{k\}}|\hskip 0.85358pt,~~~~~\widetilde{M}_{\{k\}}\!=k\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{1\}}\hskip 0.85358pt,\

(181)

where
\hskip 0.85358ptk\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, under the integer group
\mathbb{Z}
,
we have the following multiplication (additive) rule
for the mass parameters:

\widetilde{M}_{\{k_{1}\}}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{M}_{\{k_{2}\}}=\widetilde{M}_{\{k_{1}\!+k_{2}\}}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(182)

For the cubic vertex
V_{{\bf abc}}
under the multiplication
operation of the group
\mathbb{Z}
, its group indices should obey

\hskip 0.85358ptk_{\bf a}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptk_{\bf b}\!=\hskip-0.85358ptk_{\bf c}\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and the masses for the three fields in this cubic vertex
hold the group relation

\widetilde{M}_{\{k_{\bf a}\}}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt\widetilde{M}_{\{k_{\bf b}\}}=\widetilde{M}_{\{k_{\bf c}\}}\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Then, we can substitute Eqs.(
181
) and (
182
)
into the original spectral condition (
167
) and
arrive at

k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}+k_{4}^{2}\,=\hskip 0.85358pt(k_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptk_{2})^{2}\!+\!(k_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptk_{3})^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptk_{4})^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt,

(183)

where
\hskip 0.85358ptk_{j}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}\hskip 0.85358pt
with
j\!=\!1,2,3,4\hskip 0.85358pt
. This can be further simplified to give the following condition:

k_{1}\!+k_{2}\!+k_{3}\!+k_{4}=\hskip 0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt.

(184)

It holds for any finite integers

\hskip 0.85358ptk_{j}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}\hskip 0.85358pt
and its important physical implication
will be discussed shortly.

From the above proof, we deduce that
the nontrivial realization of the group (
173
)
for a physical theory [holding the mass spectral
condition (
167
)] is isomorphic to
a direct product of integer groups
\mathbb{Z}^{r}
. Thus, for a given type of particles in this theory, their mass
is characterized by the indices corresponding to each of the
subgroup
\mathbb{Z}\hskip 0.85358pt
, namely,

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\{n_{1},\hskip 1.42262ptn_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 1.42262ptn_{r}\}}
,
where
\hskip 0.85358ptr\!\!\geqslant\!\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
. Extending Eq.(
179
) under a single integer group

\mathbb{Z}
to the general case of the product group

\,\mathbb{Z}^{r}\hskip 0.85358pt
and defining an index vector

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbf{n}\!=\!(n_{1},\hskip 1.42262ptn_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 1.42262ptn_{r})\hskip 0.85358pt
,
we can express the (mass)
2
for the particles of
type-
\mathbf{n}
by the following
\,r\times r\,

real symmetric matrix:

\mathbf{M}_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}\,=\,\mathbf{n}\hskip 1.42262pt\overline{\bf M}^{2}\hskip 1.42262pt\mathbf{n}^{T},

(185)

where
\overline{\bf M}^{2}
is a positive-definite matrix coefficient. For the mass matrix (
185
),
the corresponding four-point scattering amplitudes
will obey the mass spectral condition (
167
). It is clear that among all the known consistent field theory models

only the KK theories with
\delta\,(=\!r)
extra dimensions
under the toroidal compactification could have a mass spectrum
behave exactly as in
Eq.(
185
). The matrix coefficient
\overline{\bf M}^{2}
in Eq.(
185
)
depends on detail of the KK compactification. For the case of a single extra dimension

\delta\!=\!r\!=\!1
, this matrix

\overline{\bf M}^{2}
will reduce to
a non-matrix quantity
\overline{\bf M}^{2}\!=\!1/R^{2}

with
R\hskip 0.85358pt
being the 5d radius. We further note that for the
\delta\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
case,
the condition (
184
) just means the requirement of the
KK number conservation, namely, the discretized
5th component of the momentum is conserved in the 5d space. This imposes a nontrivial condition requiring
that the 5d extra dimensional space should be
properly compactified (without
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold). For the general case of
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\hskip 0.85358pt
extra dimensions,
we can substitute Eq.(
185
)
into the spectral condition (
167
) and derive
the following conservation condition on the KK index vectors:

\mathbf{n}_{1}+\mathbf{n}_{2}+\mathbf{n}_{3}+\mathbf{n}_{4}=\hskip 0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt,

(186)

with each index vector

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbf{n}_{j}\!=\hskip-0.85358pt(n_{j1},\hskip 1.42262ptn_{j2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 1.42262ptn_{j\delta})\hskip 0.85358pt

and
\hskip 0.85358ptj\!=\!1,2,3,4\hskip 0.85358pt
.

We note that in the above proof [which leads to the unique
KK mass-spectrum (
185
) from solving the spectral
condition (
167
)], we have only made two
modest assumptions:

(i).
the particle masses in a massive field theory
are characterized by a finite number of basic parameters
(as required by a sensible physical theory);
12
12

12

We note that the validity of the spectral condition (
167
)
also requires the theory to contain at least two types of particles
with unequal masses.

(ii).
the cubic interaction vertex
V_{\textbf{abc}}

can unqiuely fix the mass
\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\textbf{c}}\hskip 0.85358pt

of the third particle
c

once the other two particles

a
and
b
are specified
(which have masses
\hskip 0.85358ptM_{\textbf{a}}\hskip 0.85358pt
and
M_{\textbf{b}}
respectively). With these we can solve the spectral condition (
167
)
and uniquely deduce that the mass-spectrum of such massive theories
has to be given by Eq.(
185
)
which is same as that of the KK theories
under the toroidal compactification. But, this does not imply that there would exist no other double-copy
constructions if the condition (
167
) does not hold. For instance, the double-copy could be realized in a more
complicated form such as the case of the double-copy construction
for the KK gauge/gravity theories under the 5d compactification
of the
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold (cf. Section
3.3
). The KK theories under such
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold compactification
do not obey the above assumption-(ii) and lead to double-pole
structure in the KK scattering amplitudes which violate the
spectral condition (
167
); so their double-copy
construction has to be realized in a nontrivial way, as we
analyzed in Section
3.3
.
13
13

13

As we clarified in the footnote-2 of Section
1
,
it is hard to directly realize the massive KLT relations
under the orbifold compactification even within the KK string
theory
KKString
, where the masses of KK open (closed) strings
are lifted by a large amount

\frac{1}{\,16\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha^{\prime}\,}
(
\hskip 0.85358pt\frac{1}{\,4\hskip 0.85358pt\alpha^{\prime}\,}
)
and the KK states fully decouple in the field theory limit
of
\alpha^{\prime}\hskip-0.85358pt\!\rightarrow\!0\hskip 0.85358pt
.
We also note that in
the 3d topologically massive Chern-Simons
gauge/gravity theories
TMG
,
all particles share
the same mass
and
thus directly violate the spectral condition (
167
)
because of
\,4\hskip 0.85358ptM^{2}\!\neq\!3\hskip 0.85358ptM^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Although the scattering amplitudes of
such massive Chern-Simons theories violate the
spectral condition (
167
), it can still
hold the color-kinematics duality in a proper massive BCJ-representation
and realize the double-copy in a different manner
Hang:2021oso
Gonzalez:2021bes
.

In addition, we note that the RS model
ExdRS
has warped 5d geometry and its compactification at IR/UV boundaries leads to the following consequences: (i). it must have a nonlinear KK mass spectrum [different from our mass-spectrum solution (
179
)]; (ii). its four-point KK amplitude contains exchanges of infinite number of KK particles. (iii). it violates the KK number conservation condition (
184
) because the RS compactification with warped 5d geometry excludes the periodic boundary conditions. Hence, the RS model violates the spectral condition (
167
), and thus cannot realize the double-copy by the existing methods.

In general, it is difficult to solve the mass spectral conditions
like Eq.(
167
). This difficult was also realized in
Ref.
Momeni:2020hmc
,
so these authors studied
Momeni:2020hmc
certain
KK-inspired massive gauge theories by assuming their mass-spectrum
to be identical to that of the 5d YM theory under the

S^{1}
compactification. By demanding the scattering amplitudes
to respect the color-kinematics duality for BCJ-like double-copy
it was found
Momeni:2020hmc

that the couplings of the 4d Lagrangian
are fixed to that of the 5d KK YM theory
together with higher derivative operators. Our above proof does not assume any KK mass-spectrum from the start,
instead we newly propose a group theory approach to

derive
the KK mass-spectrum as in Eq.(
185
) under
fairly simple and modest assumptions (explained above). Hence our approach differs essentially from the
literature
Momeni:2020hmc
.

Finally, we note that 4-point spectral condition (
167
)
is the minimal spectral condition because the case of 3-point amplitudes
has no internal propagator at tree level. Then, we discuss the general spectral condition for
the
N
-point massive scattering amplitudes including

\hskip 0.85358ptN\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\hskip-0.85358pt5\hskip 0.85358pt
. For this, we factorize an
N
-point
scattering amplitude into a 4-point sub-amplitude plus an

(N\hskip-0.85358pt\!-\hskip-0.85358pt2)
-point sub-amplitude. This 4-point sub-amplitude has a new 4th external state
which originates from breaking an internal propagator. According to the properties of the generic cubic vertex

V_{\textbf{abc}}
and of its associated group
(
173
), we deduce that the 4th external state of
this 4-point sub-amplitude has a pole-mass

\hskip 0.85358ptM_{X_{4}}\!
with its mass index given by

\hskip 0.85358ptX_{4}=k_{4}+\cdots+k_{N}\hskip 0.85358pt
. Thus, according to the additive rule (
182
)
[which has been proven by solving the
four-point spectral condition (
167
)], we can deduce

M_{X_{4}}=\hskip 0.85358ptM_{k_{4}}+\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt+M_{k_{N}}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(187)

Because this four-point sub-amplitude does hold
the spectral condition (
167
),
we can use Eq.(
167
) together
with the relation (
187
) to derive a new spectral condition:

M_{1}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\!M_{2}^{2}\!+\!M_{3}^{2}\!+\!\big(M_{4}+\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt+M_{N}\big)^{2}=\hskip 0.85358ptM_{12}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\hskip-0.85358ptM_{13}^{2}\hskip-0.85358pt+\!M_{23}^{2}\,,

(188)

where on the righ-hand side we make use of the relation

M_{1X_{4}}^{2}\!=\!M_{23}^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt
due to the conservation of the sum
of all the
N
external momenta. Hence, Eq.(
188
) just gives the new

N
-point spectral condition as generalized from the four-point
spectral condition (
167
).

It is clear that the unique solution (
181
)
to the four-point spectral condition (
167
)
is also the solution to above
N
-point spectral condition
(
188
). We can substitute Eq.(
181
)
into Eq.(
188
) and arrive at:

k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}+(k_{4}\hskip-0.85358pt+\cdots+k_{N})^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt=\hskip 0.85358pt(k_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptk_{2})^{2}\!+\!(k_{1}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptk_{3})^{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358pt(k_{2}\!+\hskip-0.85358ptk_{3})^{2}\hskip 0.85358pt.

(189)

From this, we further derive the following condition:

k_{1}\!+k_{2}\!+k_{3}\!+k_{4}\!+\cdots+k_{N}=\hskip 0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt,

(190)

which requires that the sum of the mass indices
\{k_{j}\}
for all

N
external states must vanish and be conserved. This is just a statement of the KK number conservation for the

N
-point scattering amplitudes, which is a generalization of
the conservation law (
184
) for
N\!=\!4
case. We note that the KK number conservation of
Eqs.(
184
) and (
190
) can be realized under
the 5d toroidal
S^{1}
compactification without the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold.

For the general case of
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\hskip 0.85358pt
extra dimensions, we
can substitute the mass formula (
185
) into
the spectral condition (
188
) and derive
the conservation condition on the
\delta
-dimensional KK index
vectors:

\mathbf{n}_{1}+\mathbf{n}_{2}+\mathbf{n}_{3}+\mathbf{n}_{4}+\cdots+\mathbf{n}_{N}=\hskip 0.85358pt0\hskip 0.85358pt,

(191)

with each index vector

\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbf{n}_{j}\!=\!(n_{j1},\hskip 1.42262ptn_{j2},\cdots\hskip-0.85358pt,\hskip 1.42262ptn_{j\delta})\hskip 0.85358pt

and
\hskip 0.85358ptj\!=\!1,2,\cdots\!,N
.

In passing, we also note that the literature
DC-4dx1

discussed explicitly an extended spectral condition
for the
N\!\!=\!4,5
cases
and the application for reducing the rank of the BAS kernel
for the BCJ double-copy; it found that the massive YM theory and
dRGT gravity model do not obey such spectral conditions;
so it has no real overlap with our study since
it did not solve the spectral condition for either
N\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!4

or
N\hskip-1.42262pt\!=\!5
to determine the allowed mass spectrum
[such as our general solutions
(
179
) and (
185
)],
and also did not study the general constraints on the mass indices
[ such as our conditions (
184
) (
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
)
and (
186
) (
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!\geqslant\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
)
for
N\hskip-0.85358pt\!=\!4\hskip 0.85358pt
,
and our conditions (
190
) (
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
)
and (
191
) (
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!\geqslant\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
)
for any
N
, which
can impose crucial constraint on the KK compactifications of the
higher dimensional gauge/gravity theories
as we have demonstrated ]. It focused on the applications to the massive YM theory and
dRGT gravity model with possible constraints
by the spectral conditions of
N\!=\!4,5\hskip 0.85358pt
.

6
Conclusions

The Kaluza-Klein (KK) compactification
KK
of higher dimensional
gauge and gravity theories predicts an infinite tower of
massive KK excitations for each known particle of the
Standard Model (SM), and has been a major frontier for
new physics beyond the SM,
including the string/M theories
string
and
the extra dimensional field theories with large or small
extra dimensions
Exd0
Exd
ExdRS
. The double-copy construction between the scattering amplitudes
of the gauge bosons and gravitons has pointed to fundamental clues
to the deep gauge-gravity connection. It has also become a powerful
tool for efficiently computing the highly intricate
scattering amplitudes of spin-2 gravitons. Especially, the massive KK graviton scattering amplitudes are
even more involved (with large energy cancellations)
Chivukula:2020
Kurt-2019
Hang:2021fmp

and the
N
-point longitudinal KK graviton amplitudes have
their leading energy dependence nontrivially cancelled down
by a large power factor
\hskip 0.85358ptE^{2N}\!\!
(
N\!\geqslant\!4
)
up to any loop order
Hang:2021fmp
. Hence, it is compelling to further study the double-copy constructions
of the massive KK graviton scattering amplitudes from the massive
KK gauge boson scattering amplitudes.

In this work, we systematically investigated the structure
of massive scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons
and KK gravitons under toroidal compactifications. For this, we proposed a shifting method
to construct the massive KK amplitudes from
their massless counterparts in the
noncompactified higher dimensional theories
and used this to establish a correspondence
from the conventional massless BCJ double-copy
to the extended massive KK double-copy (Section
2
). Then, using this shifting method
we studied the massive KK double-copy constructions
via both the extended BCJ approach (Section
3
)
and the extended CHY approach (Section
4
)
as well as their quantitative connections
to the massive KLT relations. We further solved the four-point
and
N
-point mass spectral conditions,
and demonstrated that KK theories under toroidal compactification
provide the unqiue solution to such mass spectral conditions
and thus guarantee successful realization of the extended
massive gauge/gravity double-copies (Section
5
). We summarize our findings in more detail as follows.

In section
2
,
we constructed an extended massive double-copy approach for
the scattering amplitudes of KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons
under the toroidal compactification within the
quantum field theory (QFT) framework. For this, using the exponential eigenfunctions of
the Laplace operator, we derived the massive KK scattering amplitudes
of a higher dimensional theory under toroidal compactification
by replacing the extra-dimensional
momentum-components in the corresponding massless amplitudes of
the noncompactified theory by their discretized values. With this shifting method, we built up a
correspondence between the conventional massless BCJ double-copy
formulation and the extended massive KK double-copy formulation. We gave in Eqs.(
15
) and (
24
)
the shifted
N
-point massive KK gauge boson/graviton
amplitudes under the toroidal
S^{1}\hskip-1.42262pt
compactification. We stressed the importance of using the toroidal compactification
without orbifold as the
base construction

of the massive KK double-copy, with which the double-copy constructions
in other KK theories under orbifold compactification (such as
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
) can be formulated by proper transformations of the external KK states. We gave in Eqs.(
18
) and (
27
)
the shifted
N
-point massive KK gauge boson/graviton amplitudes
under the orbifold compactification of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

In Section
3
,
we presented the extended four-point massive BCJ-type
double-copy construction of scattering amplitudes
of the KK gauge bosons and KK gravitons under the 5d toroidal
compactification with or without orbifold. In Section
3.1
,
under the toroidal compactification without orbifold and
by requiring the double-copied KK graviton amplitudes
(
39
) being invariant under the
generalized massive gauge transformation (
34
),
we derived a four-point mass spectral condition (
41
). Then, we deduced a massive fundamental BCJ relation
(
49
) for the KK gauge boson amplitudes and
applied it to derive a spectral condition (
52
)
which fully agrees to what we derived in
Eq.(
41
) by using the generalized
massive gauge transformation (
34
). We showed that the spectral condition (
41
)
can serve as a necessary and sufficient condition to ensure
a consistent double-copy of the KK gauge boson/graviton
scattering amplitudes. Using the massive fundamental BCJ relation
(
49
), we also proved that
the four-point massive BCJ-type double-copied KK graviton amplitudes
(
50
) [(
39
)] can be
derived from the massive KK KLT relation (
47
)
(which was based on our previous derivation of the KK
open/closed string amplitudes
KKString
), and thus
they are equivalent within the QFT.

In Section
3.2
, we systematically derived the
explicit double-copy constructions for various
four-point elastic and inelastic KK graviton scattering amplitudes
at tree level from the corresponding KK gauge boson amplitudes
under the 5d toroidal compactification of
S^{1}
. Here we used the effective leading longitudinal polarization tensor
(
53
) of the KK gravitons to derive the double-copied
massive KK graviton amplitudes which take rather compact forms
of the BCJ-type. Then, we used the exact longitudinal polarization tensor
(
25
)-(
26
) to compute
the corresponding full four-point KK graviton scattering amplitudes
which are presented in Appendix
C
. We demonstrated in
Eqs.(
79
)-(
80
)
that under high energy expansion,
each leading-order (LO) scattering amplitude of longitudinal
KK gravitions computed by using the simple effective leading
longitudinal polarization tensor (
53
) always
equals the LO amplitude computed by using the exact longitudinal polarization tensor
in Eqs.(
25
)-(
26
). From Eqs.(
79
)-(
80
),
we derived a nontrivial sum rule condition (
81
)
and made an important conclusion stating that each
N
-point
longitudinal KK graviton amplitude at the LO (with two or more
external KK graviton states being longitudinally polarized)
can be constructed by double-copy of a single amplitude of KK
gauge bosons (in which the corresponding KK gauge bosons
are longitudinally polarized only) according to the effective leading
longitudinal polarization tensor (
53
). Then, in Section
3.3
we extended the analyses of
Section
3.2
to the case of the 5d orbifold compactification
of
S^{1}\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. We demonstrated explicitly that all the four-point
double-copied KK graviton scattering amplitudes under orbifold compactification can be derived from the corresponding
KK graviton partial amplitudes under
S^{1}
compactification
by transformations of the external KK state, and thus are expressed
in terms of proper combinations of the corresponding KK
graviton partial amplitudes of Section
3.2
.

In Section
3.4
, we further constructed explicitly
the massive five-point KK graviton scattering amplitudes by the
extended BCJ double-copy formulation using the shifting method,
as shown in Eqs.(
3.4
)-(
97
)
and (
3.4
). In Section
3.5
, we studied the scattering amplitudes
of super massive KK states in the nonrelativistic limit. We first derived the nonrelativistic amplitudes of the KK gauge bosons
and of the KK gravitons in Sec. 3.5.1. We found that
the LO elastic amplitudes of the KK gauge boson and of the KK graviton
exhibit a low energy behavior of
1/Q^{2}
due to the exchange of
massless zero modes in the
t
and
u
channels, as shown in
Eqs.(
102a
) and (
3.5
). After Fourier transformation, this
1/Q^{2}
behavior reproduces the
classical Coulomb potential
1/r
(for the elastic KK gauge boson
scattering) and the classical Newtonian gravitational potential
1/r

(for KK graviton scattering) in nonrelativistic limit. In Sec. 3.5.2, we further considered a compactified 5d
Einstein-Scalar theory (with scalar field
coupled to gravity) in Eq.(
107
) and a compactified
5d Einstein-YM theory or Einstein-Maxwell theory
(with gauge fields coupled to gravity)
in Eq.(
113
). We derived the four-point scattering amplitudes
of KK scalars and of KK gauge bosons through exchanges of the zero-mode graviton, the KK gravitons, and radion. We computed both the elastic and inelastic amplitudes and derived
their behaviors in the nonrelativistic limit. The nonrelativistic elastic KK scalar amplitudes are given in
Eq.(
112
), whereas the nonrelativistic elastic KK
gauge boson amplitudes are given in
Eqs.(
117
) and (
120
). They exhibit different angular behaviors in the low energy
nonrelativistic limit. Their LO nonrelativistic
elastic amplitudes are given by Eqs.(
112a
),
(
117a
), and (
120a
),
respectively. We found that these LO nonrelativistic elastic amplitudes
have a massless pole due to exchanging massless zero modes in
the
t
and
u
channels. After Fourier transformation into the
coordinate space, they recover the classical Newtonian gravitational potential
1/r\hskip 0.85358pt
.

In Section
4
,
by generalizing the conventional massless CHY method,
we presented an extended massive CHY formulation for
the KK scattering amplitudes under the 5d toroidal compactification. In Section
4.1
,
we presented the extended massive KK CHY scattering equations in
Eqs.(
122
)-(
123
). We derived the massive KK KLT-type double-copy relation
(
128
) and formulated the massive KK graviton
scattering amplitude
\mathcal{M}_{\hskip-0.85358ptN}(\{\mathfrak{n}\}\hskip-0.85358pt)
as products of
the two color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitudes

\mathcal{A}_{N}([\alpha],\!\{\mathfrak{n}\}\hskip-0.85358pt)
and
\mathcal{A}_{N}([\beta],\!\{\mathfrak{n}\}\hskip-0.85358pt)
,
together with a kernel
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}([\alpha|\beta],\!\{\mathfrak{n}\}\hskip-0.85358pt)\hskip 0.85358pt

which is interpreted as the
inverse of the massive KK bi-adjoint scalar amplitude. In Section
4.2
, we presented the extended CHY construction for
massive KK bi-ajoint scalar amplitudes, which are given by the
formula (
132
) and can be computed according to
the massive KK scattering equations (
122
)-(
123
). We derived explicitly the four-point elastic KK bi-adjoint scalar
amplitudes in Eqs.(
137
)-(
140
)
and the inelastic KK bi-adjoint
scalar amplitudes in Eqs.(
4.2
)-(
143
)
for illustrations. Then, we presented the five-point massive scattering amplitudes of
KK bi-adjoint scalars in Eq.(
145
)
which can form a rank-2 kernel matrix. In Section
4.3
, we presented the extended CHY construction of
the massive KK gauge boson amplitudes and KK graviton amplitudes
in the compactified 5d YM and GR theories. We derived in Eq.(
151c
) the BCJ-type double-copy
construction of the KK graviton amplitude by the product of
KK gauge boson amplitudes including a KK bi-adjoint scalar kernel

\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}([\alpha|\beta],\!\{\mathfrak{n}\}\hskip-0.85358pt)

given by Eq.(
152
). We further converted this into the massive KLT formulation of the
KK graviton amplitude in Eq.(
153
), in which
the KLT kernel
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathcal{K}([\alpha|\beta],\!\{\mathfrak{n}\}\hskip-0.85358pt)\hskip 0.85358pt
is given by
the inverse of the KK bi-adjoint scalar amplitude

\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\hskip 0.85358pt\mathrm{BAS}}_{N}([\alpha|\beta],\!\{\mathfrak{n}\}\hskip-0.85358pt)

in Eq.(
152
)
and the color-ordered KK gauge boson amplitude

\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{YM}}_{N}\big[\alpha,\hskip-1.42262pt\{\mathfrak{n}\}\big]

is given by Eq.(
154
). These explicitly build up the connections of the massive KK CHY
formulation with the massive KK BCJ approach
(shown in Section
3
)
and the massive KK KLT relations (shown in the current
Section
3
and in Ref.
KKString
).

In Section
5
, we studied the possible solutions to the
mass spectral condition (
167
)
of the four-point KK scattering amplitudes
and to the mass spectral condition (
188
)
of the general
N
-point KK scattering amplitudes. For this we newly proposed a group theory approach to prove that this
four-point spectral condition uniquely determine the allowed mass
spectrum to be that of the KK theories under toroidal
compactification with the conservation of KK numbers. In our proof, we identified the group structure underlying the
spectral condition (
167
) is a product of
integer groups
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}^{r}
(with rank
\hskip 0.85358ptr\hskip 0.85358pt
)
in the finitely generated Abelian group of Eq.(
173
). Then, we proved that the unique
solution to the spectral condition (
167
)
is given by Eq.(
179
) for the case of

\hskip 0.85358ptr\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
and by Eq.(
185
) for the general case of

\hskip 0.85358ptr\!\geqslant\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
. By inspecting all the known consistent
QFTs, we concluded that

only the KK theories with
\delta\,(=\!r)
extra dimensions
under the toroidal compactification could have a mass spectrum
behave exactly as in
Eq.(
185
). Moreover, we found that the spectral condition (
167
)
has imposed a nontrivial condition on the conservation of the
mass indices (associated with the group
\hskip 0.85358pt\mathbb{Z}^{r}
), as shown in
Eq.(
184
) for the case of
\hskip 0.85358ptr\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt

(corresponding to a single extra dimension
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
),
or as shown in Eq.(
186
)
for the general case of
\hskip 0.85358ptr\!\geqslant\!1\hskip 0.85358pt

(corresponding to
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!=\hskip-1.42262ptr\!\hskip-0.85358pt\geqslant\hskip-0.85358pt\!1\hskip 0.85358pt

extra dimensions). These just single out the KK theories under the
toroidal compactifications without orbifold
(which can conserve the discretized extra-dimensional momenta and
thus KK numbers) as the unique realization of the spectral condition
(
167
). The general
N
-point spectral condition (
188
)
also imposes the KK number conservation
as in Eq.(
190
) for the case of
\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!=\!1\hskip 0.85358pt

and in Eq.(
191
) for the case of

\hskip 0.85358pt\delta\!\geqslant\!1\hskip 0.85358pt
.

Finally, for the analyses in the main text, we also provided
in Appendix
A

the kinematics of the four-point scattering for both the massless
5d theories and the compactified massive 4d KK theories. Then, we presented the kinematic numerators for KK gauge
boson scattering amplitudes in Appendix
B
, and the double-copied full scattering amplitudes of KK gravitons
in Appendix
C
.
